- [x] The game froze for 1 to 3 seconds now and then when a fish was hooked or landed, at the lake and at sea (Oscar, 2026-11-19; branch `fish-shader-warm`). The cause: a fish's model was built on the frame it was hooked, and the first of each kind (plain, glossy from tier 2, glow-skinned, ghost, each deep sea finish) compiled its shaders on that frame, since the warm-up only covers what is in the scene when a map is built. Once per kind per visit, again at sea, and only slow with nothing in the browser's shader cache, which every change to shader code empties, so it looked random
  - Found by four agents (one measuring in headless Chrome on Opus, three reading the cast path, the landing path and the server on Sonnet), their reports checked against the code. Measured on an M1 with a cold cache: 0.3 to 1.3 s per kind on a calm machine, 1.7 to 5.1 s on a busy one, 0.6 to 2.0 s at sea; the second fish of a kind 0 ms. The server, saving, sounds, icons, lights and disposal were ruled out
  - **The fix** (`Warmups` in `src/game/prewarm.ts`, on `World.warmups`; GAME_DESIGN.md §15): nothing built during play draws before its shaders are ready. The fish on the line is built when the bobber lands, hidden, and compiles in the background during the wait for the bite (as does its shadow); it shows from the hook on, or the moment it is ready if that comes later. A fish or tin taken in hand, another player's catch, a dropped item and a board's trophy stay hidden until theirs are ready. With programs already made, the usual case, nothing is compiled or drawn and the model shows a frame or two later
  - Two things the first version left (100 to 150 ms for the Hundred-Eye Lurker, which has five programs): `compileAsync` only waits on a material's current program, so every program is waited on; and three.js asks a program for its log and uniforms the first time it draws with it, which waits on the draws queued before it, so each is asked before anything is drawn with it (`World.programsReady`)
  - Checked with the investigation's probe, cold cache each time: landing every fish, 4.0 to 7.5 s of freezes in total before and no frame over 50 ms after; 30 of 30 lake loops online beside a second player with nothing at the hook (one 183 ms skip during a wait, the main thread free); 24 of 24 sea loops in the Drop-off and Kelp Deep with nothing while fishing (the sailing out and back still compile behind their cards, as before). Joining is no slower (`npm run bench:load -- --online`, 79 programs both ways). Tests (1143, seven new for `Warmups`), build, smoke 126 of 126
  - On the SwiftShader stand-in (software rendering, about 1.3 s a frame at rest in this run) a new fish no longer blocks the game's own code (2 to 10 ms where it was 1.6 to 4.5 s); the renderer itself still falls behind while it compiles (1.3 to 2.6 s gaps against 1.6 to 4.5 s before), so no worse there, and not a measure of a real phone
  - Not checked: a real phone, Firefox (no background compiling there, so the wait moves from the hook to the bobber landing; filed), Oscar's play test. Filed in Ideas: the catch card's frame or two on lake landings, the depth fade making fish shaders slower to compile, the same early query for the join screen's warm-up, and three one-off stalls that never repeated. No What's new entry (a fix)

- [x] Far lamps' light on the ground no longer shows an outline at night (Oscar, 2026-11-19, with screenshots of the pier root, the street and the quay by the seaplane: "a clear outline of the artificial lighting"; branch `night-pool-edges`)
  - Why it showed: a far lamp's light is a pool drawn on the ground (`src/game/nightglow.ts`) until the player comes near enough for its real light. Each pool was a square 0.6 of the light's range across each way, but its light only fades to nothing at the full range, so it stopped along straight lines at about 5% of its middle brightness. The pools also added their light after the screen's encoding, which inflates a faint tail (0.8% of linear light showed as about 22 of 255 on dark ground), so they read bigger and brighter than the real lights that replace them. Three more: a pool from a lamp on a dock was clipped to the planks, so the sand round the quay's edge lamps and Reed's lantern stayed dark until the real light came on; a pool draped on a 1 m grid cut under banks and the cobbles' edges and up the side of a dock, leaving hard-edged holes; and a ground lamp's pool lit the planks at the ground's strength, about 11 times too bright
  - Now each pool fades out before its mesh's edge; it adds what the lamp would add on screen through the scene's own tone mapping and encoding over moonlit ground (`POOL_BASE`), so a faint tail sinks into the dark as a real light's does; it has a layer draped over the terrain as drawn (`terrainHeight`, a 24 by 24 grid), off at the waterline, and a flat one on the planks, each keeping to its own surface; the lamp's height is taken over the surface under each point; and the gain follows the surface, not the lamp (`POOL_GAIN`: ground 0.15, planks 0.04)
  - Checked: six night views staged headlessly (the pier root from both sides, the harbour by the seaplane, the quay's west end, the street, Reed's lane), each with every lamp a real light, every lamp a pool, and as in the game, against main, and the gains swept against the real lights; the build; the tests (1108 of 1110, the two known worktree timeouts). Not measured: the cost on a phone (the machine was under another session's load); the pool's shader does a little more per pixel (the dock test for every pool, two tone-map calls) and the mesh is about twice the triangles (about 21,000), both small against the scene. Smoke not run yet. No What's new entry (a fix)
- [x] 🎨 The lake's water and bed read as depth (Oscar, 2026-11-19: "the water and lake bed look relatively flat, like there is simply a colour filter where the water is"; branch `lake-water-depth`; mockups https://claude.ai/artifact/4cDisqaqzvdoGXWxeLRqLV, the build https://claude.ai/artifact/BzkFyNpU5pT4qJDwweammN)
  - Why it was flat: the water's colour and opacity followed distance from the camera, not the water over the bed (`waterFrag`), so ankle-deep and 6 m of water got the same film; the bed was lit like dry land, with a navy disc at -6 m whose hard outline showed through; the surface cut the shore in a hard line
  - Four looks mocked up in the running game: A "True depth", B "Sunlit shallows", B2 (B murkier), C "Living lake" (weeds, minnows, sunken things, a treeline reflection). **Oscar picked B, clarity leaning toward B2** (the two cost the same); nothing from C
  - Built (knobs in `LAKE`, `src/data/lake.ts`; GAME_DESIGN.md §8): every built-in material under the surface fades by the water over it, by world height rather than a list of meshes (`src/game/underwater.ts`): absorption along the light's path, red first, toward a greener body looking down and a pale grey-blue looking across, so posts, stones, the bobber's foot, a hooked fish, the line and other players' catches fade with depth and nothing above the water does. The surface is the mirror and glints near the viewer, thins at the shore from a baked bed map, and isn't drawn inside the rowboat (`lakewater.ts`, `hulls` in `town.ts`). On the bed: colour by depth (pale sand, weedy green past the lip, dark silt), wet sand, a soft foam line, smooth normals under water, a net of sunlight that follows the sun's refracted ray, fades on steep or slantwise slopes, past 7 to 13 m and with the sun, and 520 stones in one instanced mesh (`lakebed.ts`). Clarity: B's in the shallows, close to B2 from about 3 m down

- [x] The zone sets' fish milestones lowered (Oscar, 2026-11-19, from a play tester in the charter endgame who asked for the Twilight's 550 to drop to 300; branch `zone-set-milestones`)
  - Kelp Deep's set now unlocks at 30 / 60 / 100 fish (was 40 / 90 / 150) and the Twilight's at 100 / 200 / 300 (was 100 / 300 / 550); prices unchanged. The Twilight's middle piece moved too, so the Drift Tunic and the Jelly Locket don't unlock on the same fish
  - Why, from the charter sim (`scripts/seaeconomy.ts`, counting each zone's landed fish): casting plain, the player lands about 210 Kelp Deep and 740 to 800 Twilight fish before the next zone opens, so 150 and 550 fit; on Shiny Lures from Kelp Deep the pearls open the next zone after about 65 and 200, so the old last pieces took about 350 Twilight fish (about 2 hours) back in the Twilight. Now plain players finish each set about halfway through its leg (the Twilight's at about 2.5 h in, was 4.1 h), and lured players come back for about 35 Kelp Deep fish (15 minutes) or 100 Twilight fish (35 to 40 minutes). The Kelp Deep and Twilight emotes, which need the whole set, come the same amount sooner

- [x] 🎨 Other players walk with a bounce and a sway (Oscar, 2026-11-18; branch `avatar-walk-bob`): "make the player movement (when seen by other players) a little more natural, similar to the slight bobble when walking seen by the NPC townsfolk. this does not need to effect the first person view"
  - Built: another player's avatar used to glide. Now its body, head and outfit lift 5 cm at each step and lean 0.05 rad from side to side, the townsfolk's own numbers, shared in `src/game/gait.ts` (`gaitLift`, `gaitSway`; `Town` uses them too, so the two can't drift apart). The rod leans with the body; the name tag, a chat bubble, the pet and a puddle stay level (`Character.step` moves a group round the emote pose, `Avatar.hand` the rod). The walk cycle (`Gait`) comes from how fast the avatar is drawn moving on the watcher's screen, so nothing new is sent: under 0.5 m/s it stands, it blends in and out over about a quarter of a second, and the stride lengthens with speed (`strideAt`), about 3 steps a second at a walk and 3.7 at a sprint, where the townsfolk's fixed 1.2 m stride would be 7 to 12. The steps stop mid-jump and under an emote. An avatar that appears (a map change, back from a charter) now starts where its player is instead of gliding over from where it was last drawn. The player's own view is untouched; avatars have no arms, so there is no arm swing
  - To judge in play: the size of the bounce and sway on a player (a player moves 2 to 7 times faster than a townsperson, so the same 5 cm reads busier), and whether a sprint wants a little forward lean (not built)
  - Checked: the tests (1069 on the merged `main`, 7 new in `gait.test.ts`), the build, and `scripts/walk-check.ts` (new: two headless players on the main street, 6 of 6: level standing, the full bounce and sway at a walk at 3.0 steps a second, level again 0.75 s after the key's release, 3.7 steps a second at a sprint, steps held mid-jump and picked up on landing; it films both passes from the watcher's screen, looked at). `npm run smoke` 123 of 123 (seated avatars, emotes and jumps on two screens among them). In the branch's worktree two or three slow tests (the fan of hands in `reel.test.ts`, the kelp and twilight looks in `looks.test.ts`) timed out at 5 s in most full runs; they take about 2 s alone, the same as on `main`, the kelp one times out the same way in a worktree without this change, and `main`'s own checkout passes them all. Smoke not rerun after the rebase onto the banner colours and pier posts (no overlap with avatars). No What's new entry (polish)

Nothing to press, no reward, no message to the server: every client works out who plays from who sits where. The town's tune and the band run on the server's clock (a bar is 2.16 s, the chord is the bar's number in the round of eight), and every choice in a part comes from the bar's number, so everyone hears the same notes. A part comes in on the next bar and plays out its bar. While any part plays, the town's pick and whistle step back to 0.12 on the terrace (the drone and the crackle stay); the band is full to 6.5 m from the fire, 0.25 at 15 m (the jetty's end), 0.1 from 22 m to 42 m (the pier, the quay, the village square) and gone by the town gate at 57 m. The music switch silences it. Also fixed: a seated body floated 0.2 m over a log, so each seat now carries its top's height (`SEAT_TOP`) and the body rests on it

- [x] 💬 Quick taps on the action button no longer zoom or magnify on iPhones (Oscar, 2026-11-16, branch `ios-tap-magnify`). On iOS, tapping to reel in a fish was taken by the browser as its own gesture and magnified the tapped part of the screen
  - Cause (from the code, not seen on a device): the touch layout turned the browser's gestures off with CSS only (`touch-action: none`, `user-select: none` on `body.touch`) and `preventDefault` on pointer events. iOS doesn't honour those for every gesture: two quick taps on one spot can still zoom the page in on it (the page is laid out wide and scaled down, so there is room to zoom), and a second tap held can bring up the text magnifier
  - Fix: the on-screen controls cancel their `touchstart` and `touchend` events (`TouchControls`, `src/game/touch.ts`), which is what iOS does honour; they work on pointer events, which still fire. The top buttons (💬, 🎒) are left alone (`keepsNativeTouch`): they act on the click, which a cancelled touch never sends, and iOS opens the keyboard only from a click
  - Also: the action button's icon and label are redrawn while a finger is on them (`setAction`), so a touch that began on one ended on a node out of the page and its `touchend` never reached the controls; the button itself takes the touch now (`.t-action > *`, `src/style.css`)
  - Not covered: the in-game chat keys, the hotbar and menus, which no one has reported. If fast typing on the chat keys zooms the same way, the same two listeners go on them (Send and 🌐 excepted, which act on the click)
  - Checked: in headless Chrome's phone emulation with real touch events: two quick presses on the action button have all four touch events cancelled, the button still presses and lets go, and 🎒 still opens the bag from its click (three new smoke checks, 126/126); a test for `keepsNativeTouch`. Headless Chrome doesn't have iOS's gestures, so whether the magnifying is gone is for a real iPhone (TODO, mobile step 7)
- [x] 💬 The chat keyboard's bottom row sits clear of the iPhone's home bar (2026-11-16, branch `keyboard-clear-home-bar`). Players told Oscar the keyboard is tough to use on a phone; Oscar saw that the space bar is so close to the home bar that taps on it were taken as a swipe to another app, a swipe up to close, or (two taps) "Type to Siri"
  - Cause: the bottom row (123, 🌐, comma, space, full stop, Send) ended 26px of the touch layout above the foot of the screen, about 15 real points on a phone, inside the bottom 21 points an iPhone gives its home bar in landscape
  - A web page can't turn those gestures off: holding them back is for installed apps only, iPhones have no full-screen mode for pages, and a page added to the Home Screen keeps the bar. So the keys move out of the strip
  - Fix: the keyboard's panel runs on under the keys as an empty lip 32 real screen points tall (`KEYS_CLEAR_PT`, `keysLiftPx`, `src/game/softkeys.ts`; `--kb-lift`, set by `ChatBox` when the keys open): 54px of the layout on a phone, 38 on a tablet. The keys keep their size and move up by about 17 points; the chat lines above lose that much. On every touch device, not only iPhones: one layout
  - 🎨 Default for Oscar to keep or change: 32 points. On an iPhone in Safari the page is narrower than the screen (it stops short of the notch), so 32 comes out nearer 29 there
  - Checked: staged in headless Chrome's phone emulation at 844x390, 667x375 and 1180x820: the space bar ends 32 points above the foot on each, the keys are the same size as before, and tapped keys and spaces type. Tests for the lip's size. Smoke skipped (CSS and one style variable). Not checked on a real iPhone, which is the only place the home bar's gestures show (TODO.md, "Play it on real phones")

Space jumped and was kept from the name box, so "Big Fish" was sent as "BigFish": another account, or a new one. The password box lost spaces the same way. Since the mobile steps merged (2026-11-15)

  - Also: the server cut a long name to 16 characters after trimming, so a name cut at a space kept the space and the next login missed the account (`cleanName`, `server/accounts.ts`; only reachable from a `?name=` link, since the name boxes stop at 16)
  - A player who joined as the spaceless name in the meantime now has a second, new account under it; nothing merges the two

- [x] 💬 Dev console `/rename` (2026-11-16, branch `dev-rename`). A player changed their name as a joke and asked Oscar to put it back, which nothing could do inside the 7-day wait
  - `/rename <name> > <new name>` in the dev console changes a player's name for them, online or not (`devRename`, `server/index.ts`). The `>` splits the two names, since names can hold spaces and never a `>`
  - It is the player's own change without the wait (`AccountStore.rename` with `force`): the new name must be free, 2-16 characters and not rude; the room and the server hear "X is now Y" if the player is online; a browser that still has the old name saved follows the account; the 💗 list and the casino record board get the new name
  - The 7-day wait starts again from this change, as after any change (kept simple, and the player's own screen already says so)
  - No What's new entry: a dev tool

- [x] 💬 Abyss markers take longer (2026-11-16, branch `abyss-marker-pace`). A player at Abyss marker 70 asked, by way of Oscar, for each marker to take 50% longer to reach. Oscar chose both changes on offer:
  - Abyss levels of the Deep Sea Rod cost half as much again: from 2,625,000 coins (was 1,750,000), still 12% dearer a level (`ROD.levelCoins`, `src/data/dive.ts`). Levels already bought are kept; only the next ones cost more
  - A charter reaches the next marker every 5 landed fish, not 3 (`MARKER.every`), so a 30-fish hold climbs 6 markers, not 10, and the strip shows five pips. Reed's line, the README and GAME_DESIGN.md (the Abyss) say so
  - What the sim found (a scratch version of `scripts/abyssjourney.ts` that fishes to marker 100 buying only rod levels, skilled hand): the furthest marker moves at the pace of the rod's Abyss levels, because a marker and a level are both a tenth of a tier and a marker's pay and a level's price both grow 12%. Fish a marker alone changes nothing over hours (3 to 5 fish: 16 to 15 minutes a marker, since a hold that climbs less sits nearer the furthest marker and pays more). Dearer levels alone: 16 to 24 minutes a marker played straight, 10 to 14 with Shiny Lures. Both, as built: about 23 and 13 minutes (41% and 31% longer). 2,800,000 with five fish would give 24 and 13 (52% and 37%), 2,975,000 gives 26 and 15 (59% and 51%)
  - Master of the Deep's price (250,000,000 coins and 1,000,000 pearls) still lands where it was meant to: `npx tsx scripts/abyssjourney.ts` gives 4 to 6 lured hours after the three sets (was 3 to 5) and 9 to 13 played straight (was 7 to 10)
  - No What's new entry: a balance change
  - Checked: tests (826), build and smoke (122/122) pass; the strip's five pips fit at desktop and narrow widths in headless Chrome. Not play-tested
- [x] Speech bubbles show the whole message (Oscar, 2026-11-15; done 2026-11-16, branch `bubble-whole-message`)
  - The bubble over a player's head cut a message at 40 characters with "…" and drew it on one line. Now it shows all of it (chat's cap is 120 characters, `CHAT_MAX`), wrapped onto as many lines as it needs: lines are up to 440 canvas pixels wide (about 1.9 m in the world, some 30 letters), and once the number of lines is known they're evened out, so a bubble doesn't end on one stray word. A word wider than a line (a row of one letter, a string of emoji) is cut at the line's end, never through an emoji. The layout is `wrapBubble` in the new `src/game/bubbletext.ts`; `say` in `src/game/avatars.ts` draws it, and hangs the sprite by its foot, so a taller bubble grows upwards and its tail stays just above the name tag (and still rises with an emote, as before). A one-line bubble looks exactly as it did
  - A long message also stays longer, so it can be read: 6 s as before up to 40 characters, then 50 ms more a character, 10 s for a full 120 (`bubbleMs`). It still fades in its last second, far away and when you stand on the speaker
  - 🎨 Defaults for Oscar to keep or change after a play test: the line width (`BUBBLE_TEXT_W`, 440: a typical 115-character sentence is 4 lines; narrower is taller); the extra time for long messages (`BUBBLE_MS_PER_CHAR`, 50). Known and left alone: two players standing shoulder to shoulder who both say something long have bubbles that overlap (so did two long one-line bubbles before, which were wider, up to about 2.8 m); 120 of the widest letter makes 8 lines, about 1.4 m tall
  - Checked: build, tests (`src/game/bubbletext.test.ts`: nothing lost from a wrapped message, even lines, cut words, emoji and skin tones kept whole, the longest message chat allows, the time a bubble stays); headless Chrome with three players on the pier, one watching: a short line, a two-line one, two 115-character sentences side by side, 120 unbroken letters and 60 emoji, and the sprites' sizes, foot height and opacity over 11 s. `npm run smoke` not run (the machine was too loaded for its chat timing checks; the bubble checks it makes were made by hand here). Not played by a person yet

- [x] 🎨 📋 Emotes (Oscar, 2026-11-15; done 2026-11-16, branch `emotes`). Every account has a base four (Wave, Nice!, Yes, No); Captain Reed sells four deep sea emotes (Kelp Deep, the Twilight, the Abyss, Master of the Deep); the casino's are split off into their own item in TODO.md (Up next, "The casino emotes"). Hold F for a wheel of 8 and let go to play (hold and drag the 👋 button on touch). Every emote plays in third person, behind the player for the small ones and a slow circle for the deep sea ones, and a step, a jump or a cast ends it. Other players see it. The design round (the emote lab and every concept, the casino's included) is on the unmerged branch `emote-designs`, kept for the casino build; the review page is https://claude.ai/artifact/AcHAs7cz96w6EPAXLajZ6n
  - 1. The system and the base four (2026-11-15): the catalog with gates and prices (`src/data/emotes.ts`), bought emotes in the save, the F wheel and the touch button, the third-person camera and the player's own model, the server relay with its checks, avatars playing them, `/emote` in the dev console (`src/game/emotes/`)
  - 2. The looks from the design round (Oscar's picks, 2026-11-15 and 16), all eight wired. The base four in style A, a cream hand cuffed in the body's colour (Wave "Mitten wave" 2.4 s, Nice! "Mitten clap" 2.6 s, Yes "Glove thumbs-up" 2.2 s, No "Glove finger wag" 2.2 s), in `src/game/emotes/looks/wave.ts`, `nice.ts`, `yes.ts` and `no.ts` with the hands they share in `hands.ts`; Kelp Deep is "Seadragon dance" (9.5 s) and the Twilight is "Moonlit oarfish" (10 s), in `kelp.ts` and `twilight.ts`; the Abyss is "Rough crossing, hunted" (F6, 9.5 s, its camera 5.5 m back), in `abyss.ts` with the toy Pelican and the rules that keep the legends out of her in `pelican.ts`; Master of the Deep is "Lord of the deep, swimming" (C5: smooth jellies, small rim kelp, a smaller Dreamer; 10.5 s, its camera 6.5 m back), in `master.ts`. The legends both bring on are in `legends.ts`. Cameras further back than the usual 5 m are the catalog's `cameraDistance`. All built from `kit.ts` (easing, seeds, `freeLook`, `glowSprite`) and `meshes.ts` (the one-draw moving meshes), checked by `looks/looks.test.ts` (each port also compared frame by frame against the lab's own build)
    - To drop one in: its look in `looks/<emote id>.ts` exporting the `EmoteLook` and its length, one line in `EMOTE_LOOKS` (`looks.ts`), the emote's length and camera in the catalog (`src/data/emotes.ts`), and a row in `PICKED` in `looks/looks.test.ts`. `LookWarmer` (`warm.ts`) compiles every registered look's shaders while the page loads, so nothing else is needed for the first play to be smooth
    - What the game changes round a look, so it matches the lab: see-through parts are drawn after the water in the look's own order (`drawAfterWater`, `player.ts`); pools of light lie 6 cm up, over the town's cobbles (`POOL_LIFT`, `meshes.ts`); materials are kept between plays, geometries and textures freed (`freeLook`, which leaves the quad all sprites share); other players' name tags and chat bubbles rise with a lifted body and over hands held up over the head (`EmotePlay.over`, which Nice! sets: the tag is drawn over everything and hid the clap). `ctx.top` and `ctx.reach` are the lab's own measure (a box round the outfit, without the pet)
  - 3. Oscar's calls after seeing it (2026-11-16): the Abyss's boat rocks about 35% less (`TILT` 1.12 to 0.73, `abyss.ts`: up to about 12 degrees of pitch and 20 of roll, her heave and the sea unchanged; a new test in `looks.test.ts` pins it and checks the Kraken's arms still stay clear of her). The rod stays put away on other players' avatars while they emote
  - 4. The deep sea emotes at Captain Reed's (Oscar, 2026-11-16), in his Wardrobe tab: each zone's emote is the last tile of that zone's sets (the Abyss's after its three legend sets) and Master of the Deep has a row of its own at the foot (`emoteTileHtml`, `emoteDetailHtml`, `src/game/game.ts`). No preview and no try-on: a mystery tile (a question mark in the dark) with the name, the gate's progress ("🔒 2 / 3 pieces") and the price; the look is a surprise the first time it's played. The panel shows the gate and how far along the player is, or the price and which currency is short and by how much ("Need 3,000 ⚫ more"), or Buy; bought, the tile shows the emote's icon and "On your wheel". Buying the piece that meets a gate says so ("Bought the Holdfast Pendant · that unlocks the Kelp Deep emote, here at Captain Reed's"), and buying an emote toasts "🌿 Kelp Deep is on your emote wheel now · hold F to play it" ("tap 👋" on touch, and "Master of the Deep is unlocked too" when it is). Reed's purse line shows coins and Black Pearls. The toast and the tooltip moved out of the HUD (`index.html`) so they show over an open menu. The wheel's locked slots are unchanged
    - Gates (checked from the save on the client and the server, `emoteGateMet`, `emoteBuyError`): Kelp Deep and the Twilight need their zone set's three pieces owned, the Abyss the three Abyss legend sets with their pets (12 pieces), Master of the Deep the other three emotes. Each costs coins and Black Pearls, both taken at once on the server (`buyemote`, `server/room.ts`; `buyEmote`, `src/game/economy.ts`), which refuses it away from Reed, without the gate, short of either currency (saying which and by how much) or a second time
    - Prices (`EMOTE_PRICES`, defaults worked out from the charter sim, `npx tsx scripts/seaeconomy.ts`, with the reasoning beside them): Kelp Deep 300,000 coins and 1,000 pearls (a third of the Deep lamps, about half an hour there); the Twilight 2,000,000 and 6,000 (a quarter of the Iron keel); the Abyss 8,000,000 and 40,000 (about three hours of first-marker pearls; its gate is 43.5 million coins of pieces); Master of the Deep 20,000,000 and 150,000 (about half the 300,000-pearl hoard a play tester held at marker 32). A test keeps a zone emote's pearls at 20 to 40% of the boat part its zone earns. They're flat, so far out in the Abyss, where pay compounds 12% a marker, the gate and not the price is the bar
      - Master of the Deep raised to 250,000,000 coins and 1,000,000 pearls (Oscar, 2026-11-16, branch `master-emote-price`: "the cost should be significant for players using lures; non lure players should know to use them"; a play tester at Abyss marker 67 was making 2 billion coins a charter and proposed that price). A new sim carries a player through the Abyss's markers and the rod's Abyss levels, which `seaeconomy.ts` leaves out (`npx tsx scripts/abyssjourney.ts`): a lured player who levels the rod owns the three sets about 6 hours into the Abyss with a charter paying 7 to 26 million, so the first price was a charter or two; the new one is 3 to 5 lured hours past the sets, near rod Abyss level 25. Pay compounds by about a third a lured charter, so each doubling of the price adds about an hour. The other three prices are unchanged

- [x] Phone browsers, step 1 of full mobile support: reconnecting by itself and problem reports (Oscar, 2026-11-15: friends on Android needed to close the browser and refresh; done 2026-11-15, branch `mobile`)
  - Dropped connections (`reconnect`, `src/game/game.ts`): a socket that closes with no reason from the server shows "Reconnecting… Lost the connection. Getting you back in." and reloads once `/health` answers and the page is in view, which rejoins with the saved login (the same poll as the redeploy card, `waitForRestart`). At most 2 such reloads in 3 minutes (`autoReload`, `src/game/restart.ts`, kept in sessionStorage), and it gives up after 2 minutes, both to the old "Disconnected" banner. A reason from the server (logged in elsewhere, kicked) never reconnects, so two devices can't take turns kicking each other
  - Dead sockets (`checkAlive`, `src/game/net.ts`): a phone can wake to a socket that still reads as open but hears nothing. Silence for 150 s while the page is in view (the server sends the clock every minute) now counts as a drop and reconnects
  - Problem reports (`src/game/clientlog.ts`, `server/clientlog.ts`, `POST /clientlog`): script errors, unhandled rejections, a start failure, lost graphics, failed joins, drops and reconnects send a beacon with the browser, map, touch or not and seconds since load; the server prints one `[client]` line each (Render's Logs tab), capped at 8 a page load and 20 per address per 10 minutes, and stores nothing. The context-lost handler no longer throws where localStorage is blocked
  - Not done: the white screen on the first load itself (TODO.md, step 1b), since its cause isn't known yet; the reports should tell

Not tried on a real phone. No What's new entry (a fix)

- [x] Bug: the boards flickered where the pier and the two jetties meet the quay (Oscar, 2026-11-15; done 2026-11-15, branch `dock-seam-zfight`)
  - Cause: `World.buildDock` laid planks every 0.28 m from a rect's start until past its end, so the last plank ran up to 0.22 m beyond it. The pier and the jetties end at z = 10, where the quay begins, so their last plank lay inside the quay's first planks at the same height with the grain crossed, and the two z-fought
  - Fix: each rect's planks are fitted to it (`round(len / 0.28)` planks at an even pitch, a 0.03 m gap between them), so none passes its rect's end; the pitch changes by 2% at most (the jetties)
  - Checked: build; tests (690 pass; the account and reel-sim tests hit the 5 s timeout on a busy machine and pass on their own); headless close-ups of the pier and east jetty roots before and after

On the sill the five baits in jars in their colours (the Glow Lure and the Moon Pearl glow); inside a plank floor, a pegboard of lures, a shelf of the five line spools and a lantern hung beside Marlow, which shows lit through the window at night (play-test: its spot); lanterns on brackets either side of the window; on the street side a rack of the five rods in their tier colours; out front a LIVE BAIT chalkboard with the three cheapest baits' prices read from `BAITS`; a bait cooler against the far wall. - [x] 🎨📋 Zone sets for Kelp Deep and the Twilight, as something to work toward on the long legs (Oscar, 2026-11-14; pulled forward from the deep sea cosmetics outline, family 2; done 2026-11-15, branch `zone-sets`)
  - Looks (renders and every round on the [Abyssal Wardrobe](https://claude.ai/artifact/PNQiJfe7Z1JVoyN1g2nhcf) page): a first round of gear and fish themes (coral reef, brass helmet, lionfish; lanterns, mirror silver, star chart) felt unclear, so Oscar asked that **each set wear its zone**: flowing kelp for Kelp Deep and Avatar-like glowing jellyfish for the Twilight, in the zone's own colours as the game draws it (its peach and lavender dusk, glassy fish and soft dot-lights, not dark neon). Picked: Kelp Deep **"Kelp current"** (kelp that floats up round you as if underwater) and the Twilight **"Seed drift"** (little glowing jellies drifting round you and rising off a crown of glowing tendrils)
  - Pieces (`zonePiece`, `src/data/cosmetics.ts`; new shop `zone`): Kelp Deep, Epic: **Kelp Cap** (40 fish, 150,000 coins), **Kelp Wrap** (90, 200,000), **Holdfast Pendant** (150, 100,000). The Twilight, Legendary: **Anemone Crown** (100, 1,000,000), **Drift Tunic** (300, 1,250,000), **Jelly Locket** (550, 750,000). No pets (legend-only)
  - Numbers from the charter sim (`scripts/seaeconomy.ts`, a copy that also counts fish per zone and buys each piece once unlocked and affordable): Oscar wanted every piece unlocked, and ideally bought, during its zone's own grind. Both reference hands unlock and buy the whole Kelp Deep set before the Twilight opens (by 2.5 h careful, 1.8 h skilled) and the whole Twilight set before the Abyss (8.4 h careful, 6.6 h skilled). Buying everything as it unlocks opens the Abyss about 0.8 h later for the careful hand (8.6 h to 9.4 h), partly because the sim then fishes for coins over pearls; the sets soak up the coins that used to sit idle (most held with nothing on sale: 507k to 33k)
  - Rules (`src/game/economy.ts`): fish landed in a zone are counted from the Fishdex (`zoneLanded`: the zone's fish and legends, `dex[id].count`), so existing records count and there is no new save field (Oscar). Each sea species lives in one zone (a test pins it). A set is a silhouette at Reed's until its zone is open (`zoneSetReached`); then a piece is locked until its milestone (`zoneUnlocked`) and on sale after. Legend and zone pieces share one buy path (`reedBuyError` / `buyReedPiece`, were `legendBuyError` / `buyLegendPiece`); the server checks the unlock from the save (the `buylegend` message keeps its name for old clients). `REED_SHOPS` is `legend` and `zone`, never sold at the stall. Reed lists the sets by zone, a zone's own set before its legends' (Kelp Deep's, the Twilight's, then the Abyss's three)
  - Reed's Wardrobe tab: "🌿 Kelp Deep's set" and "🪼 The Twilight's set" each get a progress bar in the set's rarity colour ("87 / 90 fish landed in Kelp Deep", "Next: Kelp Wrap", marks at each milestone with ✔ once reached, "✔ Set unlocked" at the end); locked tiles read "🔒 90 fish" and the button "Land 3 more fish in Kelp Deep". The landing that reaches a milestone adds a line to the catch card ("🌿 Kelp Cap unlocked at Captain Reed's · 40 fish landed in Kelp Deep"), only for the player. The player's own Wardrobe also lists the zone pieces not bought yet, like the legend pieces
  - Models (`src/game/character.ts`): ported from the renders; everything that sways, rises or circles (blades, floats, bubbles, tendrils, the seven circling jellies) moves once a frame for all wearers (`animated`). Draw calls: Kelp set 9, Twilight set 19 (the Kraken set 35); animation under 0.1 ms a frame. Icons in `cosicon.ts`; `Avatars.warmLegends` now prewarms zone pieces too
  - Dev console: `/zonefish <zone 0-4 or id> <n>` sets the fish landed in a zone (spreads `n` over its plain fish, keeps legend counts, takes back pieces above `n`)
  - Defaults to check in play: the Twilight crown's tendrils read nearly white in the game (lilac on the renders page); the Drift Tunic's top jelly sometimes passes in front of the face; the milestone line on the catch card has no banner or sound (a legend piece gets a banner)
  - Checked: build, 690 tests (new: zone counts, disjoint zones, thresholds, offers, order, buy errors, server rejection of a locked buy, the milestone line firing once, `/zonefish`, icons); `npm run smoke` 113/113; headless Chrome: every piece and set front, side and back on land and at the Twilight by day, dusk and night, the Wardrobe preview and silhouettes, Reed's tab on desktop and a 844x390 touch phone before Kelp Deep opens, part way and with the Twilight open, and the catch card line. What's new entry 12, "Sets for Kelp Deep and the Twilight", with a photo
- [x] Returning players don't get the full load-hitching fix: their join card hid as soon as the server answered the auto-join (`fishing-auto`, `Game.finishJoin`), possibly before the background `compileAsync` (`World.prewarm`) was done (done 2026-11-14, branch `returning-join-prewarm`)
  - Changed: once the server lets a player in, the join card stays up saying "Loading…" (`finishJoin`) and the frame loop takes it down (`enterGame`) when the map's warm-up is done (`World.ready`, including a map the save is on, built right after the join), the `warm` compiles and their one-pixel draws are done (`World.warmedAll`, new) and the legend pieces' stand-ins are built (`Avatars.buildingLegends`, new), or after 10 s at the latest (`JOIN_WARM_CAP_MS`, a play-test default) so a stuck warm-up can't keep anyone out. The Smokehouse stage banner waits for the card too (`afterEnter`). Applies to every online join (new players usually find it done already); `?name=` links and "or play offline" are unchanged
  - Covers the related legend warm-up idea for online joins (that wait is now on the card); the offline button still hides the card at once, left in Ideas
  - Probe: `npm run bench:load` gained `--returning` (an auto-join as the page loads; "join" is when the card hides), `--casino` (a seeded account saved in Casino Cove), `--slow-compile S` (background compiles report not done until S s, a stand-in for a GPU that compiles slowly while the server answers fast) and per-run `warmedVsJoin`, `legendsVsJoin` and `loadingFor`
  - Measured (M1 Metal, heavy and varying load from other sessions, load average 4-50): on the M1 and SwiftShader the map's warm-up was already done before the auto-join answer was handled in every run (7 pond, 3 casino, 5 phone-stand-in runs on main), since the warm-up's main-thread work delays the answer; but the legend stand-ins' 4-5 programs were created 0.1 s before to 0.3 s after the card hid on the M1, so their compile landed in game (0-17 ms, 5 programs in 2 of 7 pond runs). After (6 M1 runs): "Loading…" shows 0.2-0.3 s (pond) / 0.4-0.6 s (casino), then no shader compiling in game (0 ms, 0 programs in all 6). Slow-compile stand-in at 4 s (3 runs each): before, the card hid 2.7-2.9 s before the warm-up was done, with 45-79 ms of compiling and a 100-183 ms warm-up-draw frame in game; after, it hid 40-50 ms after the warm-up, with 0 ms compiling in game and the warm-up draw on the card. Phone stand-in (5 runs each): no change from the map (done 1-14 s before the card hid either way), and the legend warm-up landed after joining in 1 of 5 runs before and 3 of 5 in a mid batch after (noise in when the answer comes); with the legend wait added, 0 of 3. The gap of 170-980 ms with no JS work right after joining is in both before and after, new players too (noted on the AudioContext idea)
  - Checked: `npm run build`, `npm test` (664). No smoke run (left for the lead after merging). No What's new entry (performance)
- [x] 🎨 Unread badge on the touch 💬 button (from "Chat on a real phone", Ideas > Mobile; done 2026-11-14, branch `chat-unread-badge`)
  - While chat is closed, every message another player sends adds to a small gold pill on the 💬 button (1 to 9, then "9+"); opening chat (tapping 💬) clears it. Only other players' messages count (`countsAsUnread` in `chatbox.ts`): not your own echo, not server notices (joins, rank-ups, renames) and not command replies, which are not conversation and would make the badge cry wolf. `ChatBox` keeps the count and reports it through `onUnread`; `TouchControls.setUnread` shows it and updates the button's aria-label. Look is a play-test default: gold fill, dark brown text, a wood-900 ring, 34px tall in the 1400px layout, top-right corner of the button (`.t-badge`, `src/style.css`)
  - Also: chat text on touch is 16px (was 14px) in the scaled layout, since it came out around 8px on a phone
  - Checked: tests for `countsAsUnread` and `unreadLabel`; headless Chrome, two `?touch` players on an 844x390 touch viewport: the badge shows 1 after one line, grows with more, a forced "9+" label fits the pill, and tapping 💬 clears it. Not checked on a real phone (still open in TODO.md). No What's new entry (polish)

- [x] A chat line when someone takes the top of the Furthest Out board (from the charter rework, 2026-11-12; Oscar, 2026-11-14; done 2026-11-14, branch `furthest-out-chat`)
  - Options offered: a line for a legend landed, for a new zone opened and for a new Furthest Out record. Oscar picked **only the new record**; the other two and the legend trophy stay in Ideas
  - The server says `🧭 Ann has sailed the furthest out: Abyss marker 12!` to everyone (a `sys` line, like the rank lines). Zones before the Abyss read `...: the Twilight!` / `Kelp Deep!`; an Abyss leader with no marker yet reads `the Abyss!`. Wording is a play-test default (`furthestLine`, `src/game/dive.ts`)
  - Rule (`isFurthestRecord`): the board's top row must move strictly past the old top: a further zone, or the same zone and a further Abyss marker. The leader pushing on counts; a tie (listed by name), a rename or a level-up doesn't; the Shelf alone never does. It fires from `Room.furthestBoard`, which already re-ranks from the saved boat parts and Abyss record (server state, so the client can't fake it). The first look after a server start only records the top, so a restart announces nothing; moves between two board ticks give one line for the final top
  - Checked: tests for the rule and wording (`dive.test.ts`) and for the line sent to everyone, with a tie, the leader pushing on and a new leader (`room.test.ts`); build and tests pass (`reel.test.ts` timed out once under load from parallel runs and passes alone). No What's new entry (small addition)
- [x] Sounds for the sea's new atmosphere (from pass 3 of the deep sea art pass, 2026-11-10; done 2026-11-14, branch `sea-sounds`)
  - Rain on the deck: two looping noise beds in `seaSound` (`src/game/audio.ts`, a soft hiss at 3.4 kHz and a lower patter at 1.1 kHz) whose level follows `rainAt`, so it starts at Kelp Deep's first markers and swells with the Twilight and the Abyss, fading over ~1 s time constants. Gull cries: while gulls are about (`gullsAbout`, the same leave/roost rule as `Gulls`) a far-off cry of 2-3 thin falling whistles, panned to a random side and low-passed, every ~9-23 s (sooner with a bigger flock). The marker buoy's bell: a soft two-note chime (E5 then C5, with a faint shimmer) once when the buoy comes alongside (`MarkerBuoy.alongside`, about 4 s after the boat stops). The pure rules are in `src/game/seamix.ts`, tested in `seamix.test.ts`
  - Defaults to check in play: rain gains 0.05 hiss and 0.03 patter at full rain (the wash bed is 0.05-0.17, wind to 0.14, so rain should sit under them); gull cry 0.018 peak, 9-23 s apart; bell 0.035 peak ringing ~2.4 s, second note 0.7 s later; "alongside" is when the buoy is within 8 m of its rest spot. Everything goes through the master gain, so Sound off mutes it
  - Checked: build, tests (665+ pass; a few timeout flakes under machine load pass alone). Not play-tested (couldn't listen); levels reasoned against the existing beds. No What's new entry (atmosphere polish)

- [x] Make the dev console login safe for screen sharing (Oscar, 2026-11-13; done 2026-11-14, branch `dev-login-prompt`)
  - Typing `/login` on its own in the chat opens a "Dev login" panel (paper card like the cast-off and updating cards) with Username and Password, both starred out (`type="password"`, autocomplete off, no `<form>`, password-manager opt-out attributes, so browsers don't offer to fill or save), Cancel and Log in; focus starts on Username, Enter sends, Esc cancels. `/resetpass <name>` with just the name opens the same panel titled "Reset password for <name>" with one "New password" box. The panel is `DevPrompt` (`src/game/devprompt.ts`, markup `#devprompt` in `index.html`); the game lets go of the pointer lock while it is open and shows the usual "Click to keep playing" after, and game keys are off while it is open (`active`). The sent command is the same `/login <user> <pass>` / `/resetpass <name> <pass>`, so the server is unchanged
  - The values never reach the chat log, echo or any history (the chat input has no up-arrow history; the panel clears its boxes on close): the echo is `/login ••••` or `/resetpass <name> ••••`. Spaces in a value are refused ("No spaces, please.") since the server splits at spaces. `/login user pass` and `/resetpass name pass` typed in full still work with the old masked echo, and `/resetpass Big Fish` (a name with spaces) is read as before (the full form is the way for those). Pure helpers in `chatbox.ts` (`secretPrompt`, `loginCommand`, `resetPassCommand`, `maskedEcho`, `promptEcho`, `secretError`)
  - Checked: tests in `chatbox.test.ts`; headless Chrome against a throwaway server with `DEV_USER`/`DEV_PASS`, desktop and `?touch` on a 740x360 touch viewport: `/login` opens the panel with focus on Username, both boxes show dots, wrong credentials give "Wrong username or password." and `> /login ••••` in the chat with no plaintext anywhere in the page, `/resetpass Tess` shows one field, Esc closes. No What's new entry (dev tool)

Each set keeps its own sparkle colour (Kraken pink, Leviathan ember, Dreamer mint). Oscar asked for the first-wear stutter to be fixed in this branch

Catch-card marks 🐉 and 💤 (`LEGEND_MARK`). Reed's tab lists the sets in the order the zones list their legends (the Abyss: Leviathan, Dreamer, Kraken; `reedCosmetics`), and after Oscar's first look it shows every set from the start: one the player hasn't reached (zone not open, legend not landed) is drawn as dark silhouettes in the tiles and the detail icon, and trying one on turns the whole preview character into a silhouette, body and head too (Oscar's second note; `legendSetReached`, `reedOffer`'s `unseen`, `Character.set`'s `shadow`), with its zone, what it takes and [Fit the boat for the Abyss], and no blurb

- [x] Check blackjack's edge (from the charter boost review, 2026-11-13; done 2026-11-14, branch `blackjack-edge-check`)
  - Measured: 10,000,000 hands through the game's own `dealBlackjack`, `actBlackjack` and `blackjackFactor` (`src/game/casino.ts`), with the best hit, stand or double worked out exactly for each hand. With best play a hand returns **+1.98%** to the player (±0.07%); at a natural paying ×2.5 it would be −0.35%, to the house (break-even about ×2.52). Standing on 17+ and never doubling returns −3.4%. The edge comes from the natural paying ×3 (2:1), the dealer standing on soft 17, a fresh single deck every hand and doubling on any two cards. A double costs coins equal to the fish and pays like a normal two-unit double, so a skilled player turns coins into fish value at those odds
  - Decided (Oscar, 2026-11-14): keep ×3. The small edge is a reward for players who learn the strategy; the casual player still loses. Nothing in the game changed
- [x] Stop the catch toasts spamming players: only announce another player's catch when it beats that player's own personal record (Oscar, 2026-11-14; done 2026-11-14, branch `catch-toast-records`)
  - Decided: another player's catch toasts only when it is bigger than that player's previous best of the species (the Fishdex record, `record` from `applyCatch`); a first catch of a species is no record, and `SaveData.best` doesn't count. The rare-or-better filter stays on top, so common and uncommon fish never announce. Shinies no longer always announce: a shiny announces only when it is also a rare+ record, and keeps its "✨ SHINY " prefix. The player's own catch card is unchanged
  - Built: the `hold` act carries `rec: true` (only when true) from `Room.land`, charter catches included (same path); `announcesCatch` in `economy.ts` is the toast rule, used by `onAct`; the `carry` act (held slot) never toasted. GAME_DESIGN.md (Shiny fish) updated
  - Checked: new Room test (first catch and an equal one carry no flag, a smaller Fishdex best gives `rec`) and `announcesCatch` tests, build, tests, smoke

- [x] Sea progress reset for everyone after the charter rework (Oscar, 2026-11-13; done 2026-11-13, branch `sea-reset`)
  - Oscar: players who played the old charters would feel disjointed with the new progression, since their old levels and furthest marker had become rod steps and boat parts and they kept their old pearls (enough to buy boat parts outright). Reset the sea without resetting accounts, rod progress and the sea's Fishdex included
  - Built: a sea save version, `seaV` / `SEA_VERSION` (`src/game/state.ts`). `migrate` resets any save without it once (`resetSea`): rod steps, Abyss levels, boat parts, the Abyss record and Black Pearls go to 0 and the sea fish leave the Fishdex; a best catch from the sea becomes the best pond record left at its plain sale value (or none). The Deep Sea Rod stays bought; coins, levels and everything at the pond are untouched. The server migrates every account on start, so one deploy resets everyone, offline accounts included, and the Furthest Out board starts empty. The old conversion (rod steps from old levels, boat parts from the old furthest marker, `OLD_LEVELS_A_STEP`, `OLD_RECORD_PARTS`) is gone, since the reset overrides it; the old fields are still dropped and the old Deep Sea Line still gives Moonsilk
  - The What's new entry 'A fresh start at sea' (id 8); entry 7's last sentence, which said old levels and pearls carried over, removed
  - Caveat: the conversion ran in the live game from today's deploy, and a converted save can't be told from one that earned its progress since, so sea progress earned between the two deploys is reset too
  - Checked: the build and 595 tests (the old conversion tests replaced: the reset, the Fishdex, the best catch, reset once and progress earned after it kept, a new save untouched). Smoke skipped: a save-load change covered by unit tests, no gameplay or scene code touched

- [x] 🎨 Tip nudge at level milestones, a follow-up to the tip jar (done 2026-11-13, branch `tip-nudge`)
  - Oscar's picks from the options shown that day: players already past a milestone see it once, after their next level-up (not on joining, not never); it opens after the milestone catch, once the fish is released and the rank banner has gone, and waits for the pond when it comes due at sea or in the casino; the short, plain wording, the same at every milestone (over a warmer one naming the rank and a different line per milestone). No What's new entry: it isn't a feature
  - Built: a small card in the What's new card's paper window, "☕ Enjoying Lakeside?": "The server costs money to run. If you'd like to help keep it going, you can leave a tip." / "Tips give nothing in game, only a 💗 by your name." with a gold ☕ Tip on Ko-fi link (new tab) and a plain Keep fishing button; Esc and ✕ close it too. It shows at most once per milestone (20, 30, 50), never for supporters and never offline
  - One rule covers the milestones and the catch-up: after any level-up, the card is due if a milestone at or under the player's level is unseen, and opening it marks all of those. So 19 to 20 shows it, a player at 34 gets one card at 35 and another on reaching 50, and a player past 50 gets it once. Back pay raises levels on the server without a catch, so a back-paid player is asked after their next level-up like anyone else
  - It opens in a lull, not at the level-up: a catch released and nothing cast since, at the pond, idle for a second (`NUDGE_BEAT_SECS`, `src/game/tips.ts`) with no menu, chat, banner or wheel spin up (`tickNudge`, `src/game/game.ts`). So it never lands in a fight, on the rank banner or after a lost fish; a player who casts again at once gets it after a later catch, at the latest on the walk to sell. One that came due on a charter opens back at the harbour, a second after the "Heading in" card has faded. The milestones (`nudge20`, `nudge30`, `nudge50`) are kept with the tips (`markTip`) and marked when the card opens, so a player who leaves before it opens is asked after their next level-up; `/tips` in the dev console clears them too
  - Added to the chosen wording: a small line under the buttons, "Write your name (<name>) in the Ko-fi message to get the 💗." Without the name a tip isn't matched to the player, who then gets no heart and is asked again at the next milestone. The Thank you board is not mentioned (the TODO had it; the short wording Oscar chose leaves it out)
  - Docs: GAME_DESIGN.md §37 (it said "Nothing pops up in play")
  - Checked: the build, 592 tests (the milestones, the catch-up, the reload and the card's text in `tips.test.ts`; the server keeping the milestones in `room.test.ts`) and smoke 113/113, with two new checks: the smoke account is seeded one XP short of level 21, so its sea fish is a level-up, the nudge waits out the charter, opens back at the harbour, Keep fishing closes it and `nudge20` is saved. In headless Chrome with a seeded server and real catches: 19 to 20 (the DECKHAND banner first, the card 5.2 s after the release, `nudge20` kept through a reload, no card on the next catch); 34 to 35 with a cast straight after the release (no card during that cast or fight, one card after the next catch is released, marking 20 and 30); 34 to 35 on a charter (no card at sea, the card a second after the "Heading in" card has faded); a supporter reaching 20 and a level-up to 13 (no card); the card on desktop and at phone size, Esc and a tap on Keep fishing closing it. Not staged: a lost fish between the level-up and the card, a charter ended with Head home (Reed's card may open first; the nudge waits for it to close), and a real pointer-locked mouse (the headless runs drive the game without one)
  - Play-test: the one-second beat; the gold link beside the plain Keep fishing; whether the card should mention the Thank you board; how it feels to be asked on the walk to sell rather than at the level-up

- [x] 📋 Deep sea charters reworked as a place to earn (Oscar, 2026-11-12; done and merged 2026-11-13, branch `charter-edge`; REELING.md, "A place to earn"; plan and mockups: https://claude.ai/artifact/9htuc63qVi88eVU9bhEp4Z)
  - Built: a charter is fished in one zone on a 300 m spool of line bought from Reed (free on the Shelf, then 14,000 to 83,000 coins); every hooked fish uses 5 / 9 / 13 m at Easy / Fair / Tough for the rod, taken at the hook, and one that gets away 25 m more; when the spool is out, another spool or head home, with nothing lost; fish pay coins (about 1,500 to 15,500 a fish by zone on average) and Black Pearls (1 / 3 / 8 / 20 / 50, double for Feisty and Brutish, five times for legends); coins buy the Deep Sea Rod's eight steps (two a zone, on sale once the zone is open) and its endless Abyss levels; pearls buy the four boat parts that open zones; only the Abyss has markers (three fish each, numbered from 1, each harder and 12% richer) and the Furthest Out board ranks by them; Reed's chart and shop, the four-cell strip, the Spool's out, head home and end cards; older saves keep their pearls and get rod steps and boat parts from their old levels and furthest marker
  - It replaced "the edge pays" (pearl water, the haul's bonus, marker starts, two level tracks), built on this branch on 2026-11-11 and play-tested the next day (REELING.md has both)
  - First play test (Oscar, 2026-11-12, on "the edge pays"), nine notes and what became of each:
    1. Reed could sell the Deep Sea Rod and Line instead of Marlow's stall → built: Reed sells the Deep Sea Rod, its steps and the boat parts; the stall's Sea gear tab is gone
    2. Reed's "Your rod and line" rows (pearl water, hard going, too much) aren't clear on a first charter: a small chart of how the gear fits the easy, matched and too-hard water instead → built: Reed's chart, a card for each zone with the rod's rating there
    3. Markers, depths and zones need to be shown more clearly or simplified → built: zones are places with no markers; markers only in the Abyss, numbered from 1
    4. The charter's on-screen UI is cluttered: simplify it together with note 3 → built: the strip shows the zone and rating, the line, this charter's coins and the next boat part's progress
    5. A cleanly landed fish still costs line (about 8 m at marker 25 on base gear, more by 30) and nothing says why: fine if it limits how far a charter goes, but it must be clear → built: every hooked fish costs a stated amount of line (5 / 9 / 13 m), taken at the hook
    6. Remove the dressing mirror between the dock and Reed's plane (the Wardrobe is on the Tab menu) → built: the harbour's mirror is removed
    7. Markers 0-30 paid about 700 pearls and 45k coins; after a line level the fish still read as too strong and too heavy and the level wasn't felt: maybe a core issue with the rod and line system → built: a rod step is 0.4 of a tier on both axes and changes the same zone (Tough to Fair to Easy)
    8. Rod plus line may be one track too many, lake and sea: the rod's tier could carry the line's strength; sea progression might then gate zones behind boat upgrades → built at sea: one rod track; the lake keeps rod and line for now, zones open with boat parts
    9. The number of fish per marker feels arbitrary and doesn't give a feeling of progress → built: no markers before the Abyss; three fish a marker there
  - Second play test (Oscar, 2026-11-13, on the rework), four notes, all built:
    1. The strip at sea wasn't centred, and phones need care (the level, coins and pearls might have to stack) → the strip is centred at the top at every size; under the level and coins on a narrow window; larger on a phone, between the level and coins and the buttons. Nothing has to stack, since the pearl count is gone (note 3)
    2. The shown coins a fish should be the average fish there, shinies and other bonuses included → `averageSale`: legends, shinies and Feisty or Brutish fish counted in (about 1,470 / 2,710 / 4,830 / 8,610 / 15,500 a fish by zone); the clean-catch bonus, snacks and charms left out
    3. Pearls only buy boat parts, so show progress to the next part instead of a currency → no pearl count beside the coins; the strip, Reed's boat row and the cards show the next part's bar ("Kelp cutter 96 / 150"), "ready" once Reed can fit it
    4. The boat parts cost too many pearls: the first after 5-10 minutes, the next 15-30 minutes on, then 1-1.5 hours, and so on → parts 30 / 150 / 1,500 / 10,000 pearls, and the rod's first four steps down to 40,000 / 90,000 / 150,000 / 350,000 coins so a new zone isn't opened into a coin wall; the sim opens the zones at about 8, 40 minutes, 2 and 5 hours
  - Also covers the Up next item "Make the charter's words and zones clear to players" (Oscar, 2026-11-01): the terms it listed are gone (charted marker, haul, Sail on, pearls growing per marker) and Reed's chart shows the five zones with what opens each. - [x] The reel fight's tier ladder, and a gauge that shows it (Oscar, 2026-11-01/02; done 2026-11-03, branch `reel-gauge`; was the 🚧 📋 🎨 at the top of Up next). Folds in three ideas: the gauge's bands showing the matchup, the green zone meaning too little in a surge (decided instead: riding the red is skill, and the gauge shows the real optimum), and fight left in the pond
  - Decided (REELING.md, "The ladder"): weight tests persistence, strength tests tension management. Over the gear, 0 is easy, +1 landable, +2 the limit (weight: the longest fight patience still wins; strength: a skilled player lands about half), +3 runs off or snaps. One ladder for pond and sea; pond fish tire like sea fish; pond fights about 10 / 25 / 60 s at 0 / +1 / +2 weight
  - Built: the gauge lab, dev only (`gauge-lab.html`, `src/lab/gaugelab.ts`; `npm run dev`, then `/gauge-lab.html`). Real fights against a concept gauge (the green's edge follows the pull, a ghost of the next surge, a blue drag tint, the red shaded by fray speed, fray pips by the red, the alarm only near a fray) next to today's; a -2..+3 weight × strength grid by fish tier, under pond or sea rules; lever sliders; skilled and careful reference hands (`HANDS`, `PUSH_STYLES` in `reelbot.ts`) on each fish and across the grid; every fight logged with its button presses and exportable as JSON for `replayFight`. In `reel.ts`: read-only helpers for the gauge (`surgePulls`, `surgeLineAt`, `markSeconds`) and `P.redFloor` as a lever
  - Done (REELING.md, "As built"): (1) Oscar's +2 bar as the baseline levers; (2) `WEIGHT_STEP` and `STRENGTH_STEP` refit, eased per tier and step (`OVER_EASE`), fray time spanning the strengths over the line; (3) every fish tires with one ±5% surge spread, fight growing as the square root of weight, and the red floor decided per fish; (4) `HANDS.skilled` fitted to Oscar's lab fights (0.12 s, letting go at the red's edge), `HANDS.best` added, +2 strength set on the best quick play (a margin below the red), the tension needle pinned at the gauge's end
  - Decided (REELING.md, "The gauge is the ladder" and "The foundation"): strength sets the pull marker against the red (0 mid-green, +1 the red's edge, +2 in the red, +3 past the end), weight sets where holding wins line (blue below), letting go in a surge gives more line the stronger the fish, the calm reels slower the heavier, and with the button up the fish always takes line. A fish is its two stats against the gear's and only the gaps count; timing is constant with no random spread, variety comes later as modifiers. The rungs are conceptual: 0 parity, +1 a slight challenge, +2 the frontier, +3 the hard line
  - Done (2026-11-02): (A) headless sim of the new rules (`GaugeReel`, `src/game/gaugereel.ts`; `scripts/gaugesim.ts` prints each axis, the grid with diagonals and each hand's frontier; anticipating and ceiling hands in `SIM_HANDS`); an outside review (Fable) whose levers were adopted; (B) the lab's **Foundation** rules, the default (gaps grid -2..+3, the gauge as Oscar sketched it, a slack-line ring, its own sliders, fights logged with gaps and levers). Oscar: the foundation to build on
  - Done (2026-11-02, REELING.md "The foundation in the game"): (C) the foundation is the game's fight. One tier is one rung (`fightGaps`); everything but the two gaps is off until wanted as a modifier; the sea a placeholder (`seaGaps`, `SEA_GAPS`); `Reel` is the foundation's fight for the game, the server's replays, the bots (`HANDS`, `handCell`) and the lab; the lab's gauge in the game (`drawFightGauge`, FIGHT bar above). 539 tests and smoke pass
  - Done (2026-11-03): the rest of (C). Oscar's 15 lab fights match the hands band by band, and his play test in the game felt right (pond tiers 1-3 on the Twig Rod and Twine; at sea, marker 35 a long fight that says "get a better rod", 45 out of reach). Settled 2026-11-03 (REELING.md): +3 strength's 3 s run-off stays; the loss card's big word says how it ended and a short line per piece of gear the fish was over names the next one; the +2.5 slog stays; one flat green; the threshold stays put as the fish tires; the hook toast and sea sticker say the matchup by rung (strong / too strong)
  - (5) The sea mapping, done (REELING.md, "The sea mapping"). Done (2026-11-03): one formula on one scale of tiers (`gapsOf`; a sea fish a tier higher every 30 fish, a level 1/6 of a tier, one marker on its gear); the spool as the price of pushing past the wall (a lost fish costs `DIVE.lostMetres` however it got away); the sea sims on the reference hands; the first modifiers, pond and sea (each fish's own ±0.15 spread on each axis, Feisty +0.25 and Brutish +0.5 one fish in 8, on any fish, the frontier too; `rollFishGaps`)
  - Modifier sequence (Oscar, 2026-11-03): (a) the spread and Feisty/Brutish, built, to play-test; (b) prototype 2-3 "how it fights" temperaments in the gauge lab, each one existing fight lever per fish and designed as a trade measured in rungs on the bots, one per fish (TODO Ideas, "Gameplay and UI"); (c) reward modifiers (Pearl fish, bosses) in the deep sea mechanics pass (TODO Ideas)

- [x] Deep sea fight left: sea fish tire, from full fight to tired to exhausted, on a bar above the reel gauge (done 2026-11-01, branch `sea-fight-left`; was the 📋 at the top of Up next)
  - Engine (`src/game/reel.ts`): a new lever `P.tire`, off by default, so pond fights and their server replays are unchanged step for step. A sea fish (`fightTune` adds `SEA_TUNE`, `src/game/dive.ts`) has 6 s of surging per unit of weight (×1.5 for a legend). Fight drains during surges against a tight line, a quarter of that against slack, and 45% of that while reeled in the calm with the gauge green. At full fight surges come ×0.8 as far apart. From 60% left it's **tired**: as it drains its pull fades to 40% (every step, so the marker slides within a surge), surges shorten to 65% and gaps grow to 1.75×. At 0 it's **exhausted**: no surges, reeled in as a fish of weight 1. A surge that reaches the red at full strength stays just inside it (`RED_FLOOR`), so tiring never beats the line. Sea surges pull within ±5% of each other (`powerJitter`), and Thrashers lost their ±40% uneven surges. The old stamina trim stays for the pond.
  - HUD (`src/game/game.ts`, `drawGauge`): a bar above the gauge labelled FIGHT, orange at full fight, yellow once tired, empty once exhausted (Oscar kept the stage words off it, so "tired" can't read as the player), glowing softly while the fish surges against a tight line; REEL IT IN! and "The fish has no fight left" once it's done. The sea sticker (`fishTagHtml`, `src/game/divehud.ts`) moved under the gauge and stays up all fight with the pull roll's word and the behaviour ("💪 Strong · 💥 Thrasher"), red with "too strong for your line" when its surges reach the red.
  - Messages (from Oscar's recording of a Kelp Deep fish that felt random): a run-off on a fish over the line now names the Deep Sea Line, not the Aurora Rod (`seaLossVerdict`, `src/game/tips.ts`), and the drag caption names the line first.
  - Spool: `DIVE.metresPerLine` 10 → 15 to keep the frontier (fast / careful bots on base gear: fish 44 / 34 before, 45 / 39 after; within a fish or two at every gear level tried). Careful players' slowest 1 in 10 fights at markers 30-45 went from over 2 minutes to under 40 s. Pearls an hour rose 2-14% fast, 3-36% careful; prices left alone. Numbers and findings in REELING.md, "Fight left (2026-11-01)", with the sims kept as `scripts/seafights.ts` and `scripts/seaeconomy.ts`.
  - Oscar shaped it over five play-test rounds: three stages instead of one spent state; tired surges fading in strength, length and frequency, gradually; fish also tiring while reeled in; a steady pull marker; the glow kept subtle in the bar's own colours; the label kept to FIGHT; the sticker moved off the top of the screen. Checked: unit tests for the rules (`dive.test.ts` "fight left", `tips.test.ts`, `divehud.test.ts`, the room tests replaying with `fightTune`), `npm run smoke` 98/98, and headless shots of each HUD state. What's new entry 2 with a staged photo.

- [x] Players online during a redeploy keep their progress and reload into the new build (done 2026-11-01, branch `redeploy-reload`; was a 📋 under Accounts and server)
  - Server: `server/shutdown.ts` `createShutdown` runs on SIGTERM/SIGINT (wired in `server/index.ts`). It sets `down` (new sockets are closed with 1012, and the `message` handler ignores everything), then for every open socket sends `{ t: 'restart' }` (new `ServerMsg`), calls `endSession` synchronously (so `Room.leave` settles spins and blackjack and marks the account changed) and closes with code 1012, and only then does `markDirty` + `flush`, a 150 ms settle for the close frames, and `exit`. A second signal is a no-op. `/health` now answers with `access-control-allow-origin: *` so a client served from another origin (dev, or a separate host) can poll it.
  - Client: `NetClient` (`src/game/net.ts`) fires a new `onRestart` (once) on `restart` or a 1012 close; any other close still goes to `onClose`/`onFailed` exactly as before (logout, "logged in somewhere else", real outages). `Game.startRestart` (`src/game/game.ts`) shows the `#updating` card ("Lakeside is updating…", "Your progress is saved. Back in a moment.", in `index.html`/`style.css`, the Casting off card's look over a plain sky), pauses play (`setCovered`, the frame `tick` is skipped, `active` is false, nothing saves locally) and polls `/health`. Pure logic and knobs live in `src/game/restart.ts` (`isRestartClose`, `restartReady`, `waitForRestart`).
  - Not reloading into the dying server: the first poll waits 1.5 s, and an OK counts only once the socket has closed AND either an earlier poll failed (the old server really went away) or 4 s have passed since the notice (it exits well within that once its sockets are closed). On a platform where the new instance is already up the 4 s rule is what lets the reload happen.
  - Decisions: a fight on the line is lost (accepted); no old-build version check, since the game server serves the client, so a redeploy always means a disconnect. If the server isn't back after 2 minutes the card goes and the usual "Disconnected" banner shows. Play-test points: the card wording (default), and the 4 s settle time.
  - Knobs: `RESTART_POLL_MS` (2 s), `RESTART_FIRST_POLL_MS` (1.5 s), `RESTART_SETTLE_MS` (4 s), `RESTART_GIVE_UP_MS` (2 min), `RESTART_PROBE_TIMEOUT_MS` (1.5 s) in `src/game/restart.ts`.
  - Checked: 520 tests (new `server/shutdown.test.ts` and `src/game/restart.test.ts`), all three tsc configs, and `npm run smoke` 98/98. The new smoke check SIGTERMs the server with a player online, checks the card shows and the offline banner doesn't, restarts the server, and checks the page reloads back in connected with the same coins. The existing offline check now SIGKILLs the server, since SIGTERM is a redeploy. Looked at the card in `.bench/smoke/updating.png`.

- [x] The smoke test no longer leaves its server on port 8788 after a timeout (done 2026-11-01, branch `smoke-server-leak`; was part of an idea under Bugs and tooling)
  - The game server now starts in its own process group (`detached: true`) in `scripts/smoke.ts`, `screenshots.ts`, `trailer.ts` and `hitch.mjs`. Each script has one `stopServer()` that signals the whole group (`npx`, `tsx` and the server): SIGTERM, then SIGKILL if it's still up after 2 s. It runs from every exit path, including the `finally` blocks and the offline check's stop, and a last-chance kill on any `exit`; Ctrl-C, SIGTERM and SIGHUP stop the server before the script exits.
  - Checked: `tsc -p tsconfig.scripts.json` and `node --check` pass. A throwaway script on port 8799 copied the logic: after `stopServer()` nothing listens and no processes are left; after SIGINT the script exits 130 with nothing left. The old `server.kill()` (SIGTERM to `npx`) did not leave the server up in that run, so the leak itself wasn't reproduced; `npx` passes SIGTERM on. Not covered: the smoke script itself being SIGKILLed, since no handler runs. `npm run smoke` not run.

- [x] A bad message can't take the server down (2026-11-01, branch `server-msg-guard`; was an idea under Accounts and server)
  - `server/guard.ts`: `guarded` wraps `Room.handle` in `server/index.ts` and logs a throw with the player id, name and message type (never the payload), so one packet can't restart the game for everyone.
  - The `Math.floor(Number(...))` index reads (`slot`, `sell`, `wheel`, `roulette`, `bj`, `cashout`, `color`, the join colour) and `pickup`'s `Number(msg.id)` now use the numbers-only `int()`, so `{ toString: null }` is just NaN.
  - No process-level `uncaughtException` handler on purpose: past the per-message guard a throw could leave state half-written, and staying up would then save it; a crash restarts from the last good save.
  - Checked: `server/guard.test.ts` (hostile id into each fixed handler, throwing handler into the guard; fails without the fix), `npm test`, both `tsc` projects, `npm run smoke`.

The Settings link only shows once there are entries.

- [x] The Lucky Wheel moved into the casino with new odds (done 2026-10-31, branch `casino-wheel`; asked for by Oscar). Some new players got stuck at the pond's wheel gambling away their fish; the Smokehouse is now the way to get more for a fish early on, and the wheel waits for level 15 like the rest of the casino. Decided (Oscar, 2026-10-31, from three wedge tables, three spots and two looks): the "Balanced" table, the back corner on the roulette side (back right as you walk in, across the hall from the record board), and a casino restyle.
  - Odds: 12 equal wedges, `WHEEL_WEDGES` in `src/data/casino.ts`: ×2, ×2, ×3, ×4 and 8 LOSE, spread round the wheel. A spin wins 1 in 3 and returns 11/12 on average (the house keeps ~8%, between roulette's red at ~5% and green at ~16%; the old wheel was 45% to double, a 10% edge).
  - Server: the pond's `gamble` message is gone; `wheel {i}` bets the fish in slot i at the casino's `wheel` spot, like roulette (`Room.wheel`, `cas.spinWheel`, `cas.settleBet`), and sends back `wheel {wedge, factor, ms}`. Results still go on the record board as "Lucky Wheel".
  - Client: `buildLucky` in `src/game/casinohall.ts`: an upright disc on a gold stand with a gold rim, a glowing halo, a gold pointer, a neon LUCKY WHEEL sign and a neon floor ring. Wedges are coloured by payout (×2 green, ×3 blue, ×4 gold, LOSE in two dark reds). Stand at it holding a fish and click the disc to spin, like the tables; the result uses the tables' banner. The pond's wheel, its E prompt and the offline local roll are gone, and so is the `#wheelres` banner.
  - Wording: the Smokehouse is now "across the path from Marlow's stall" (a tip, the news line, the Bag's empty smoked list, README), the loading tip points to the casino, and the casino poster's text names the Lucky Wheel. GAME_DESIGN.md's Lucky Wheel section is rewritten.
  - Checked: build, tests (451, with new ones for the wedge table and its odds, the server spin at the casino, the pond having no wheel, and the record board), and `npm run smoke` (92/92; the pond wheel check is replaced by two casino checks: spin with a fish from `/fish`, ticks, then the fish settled). Headless screenshots from the door, mid-floor and in front of the wheel. Re-shot the casino poster (`casino-preview.jpg`), which now shows the wheel; the README pond shots never showed it, so they weren't regenerated.
  - Play-test points: the wheel's size (it reads smaller than the tables from the door). The old spot by the Smokehouse is left as open sand.
  - Follow-up from Oscar's play test (2026-10-31, branch `casino-wheel-fixes`): the spot is right. Colours redone (Oscar picked "roulette house" from three): LOSE black, ×2 in the tables' felt green, ×3 in roulette red, ×4 gold, gold numbers with a dimmed LOSE, and thin gold lines between wedges (`WEDGE_INK` / `WEDGE_TEXT`, `casinohall.ts`). The wheel no longer rises when aimed at (the bet patches and buttons still do). At every table the fish now stays in your hand until the bet is placed, and leaves it only while the spin or hand plays out (it used to vanish as soon as you walked up). E now plays at the tables, with a prompt (`tablePlay`, `game.ts`): it spins the wheel or deals blackjack with the fish in hand, and otherwise plays what you aim at (a roulette colour, hit, stand, double); clicking still works. The table readout drops its "Click: …" line, since the prompt shows it. Oscar asked for ×2 as common as LOSE with a rare ×4, then for one or two ×½ wedges. Since every win at least doubles, those would pay players more than they bet (117% on average), so from three fair-or-near layouts Oscar picked: 6 LOSE, 2 ×½, 3 ×2 and a ×5 (`[0, ½, 0, 2, 0, 2, 0, ½, 0, 2, 0, 5]`), a win 1 spin in 3, exactly fair (100%). `settleBet` now applies any factor, so ×½ halves the fish; it shows a gold "×½ · HALF BACK" banner with the short jingle and counts as a loss of half on the record board. Colours after two looks: LOSE black, ×½ charcoal with dimmed gold text, ×2 roulette red, ×5 felt green, gold numbers. The E prompt now reads "Spin the wheel with … · up to ×5". Checked: build, tests (453, with the wedge mix, no neighbours alike, and ×½ halving), `npm run smoke`, re-shot casino poster.
