- [x] D1, dive rules (done 2026-10-31, branch `d1-dive-rules`): the sea dive's rules as pure logic, not wired into the game yet (D2 runs the dive on the server, D3 shows it).
  - Built: `src/game/dive.ts` (rules) and `src/data/dive.ts` (zones, spool, pearls, haul). Sea difficulty is 1.6 × 1.01^depth × 0.97^rod level × zone factor × stamina / 2.6, no clamp, powers by multiplication. Shiny sea fish are worth more, not harder. A bite is the zone's legend 1 time in 20, else one of its two everyday fish; Kelp Deep and below borrow the Drop-off's fish until theirs exist. The spool loses 10 m per fight length of line taken (`wear.lineOut`), 25 m a snap and 5 m a fray left on a landed fish; running out sinks the haul, but the landed fish come home. A ledge every 5 depths, haul +10% a ledge reached (cap +50%), pearls 1 + floor(depth / 20), charting and the record on surfacing.
  - Behaviours are reel levers (`BEHAVIOUR_TUNE`, through `new Reel(d, rng, tune)`): Runner (Drop-off) takes more line, Thrasher (Kelp Deep) surges short and often, Sounder (Twilight) gives little warning and frays sooner; the Abyss mixes all three with a ×1.05 zone factor. Each lands about 4-6% less far than a plain fish for 0.25 s and 0.7 s bots (`dive.test.ts`).
  - P1 rerun (scratch, the real reel and feathering bots): frontiers hold within 5 depths (q50 50 / 80 / 110 / 165 at rod +0 / +10 / +20 / +40 for 0.25 s, 25 / 50 / 80 / 145 for 0.7 s). Pearls an hour run 1.2-2× P1's, mostly because F1's easy fights are shorter, so levels come every 26-44 min. Under P1's natural play the slow bot overtakes the fast one in rod levels after ~20 h, since a fast player's frontier fights last 20-45 s against ~10 s in easy water; that makes the pearl bonus for charting a new deepest ledge (Ideas) worth pricing in D4.
  - Oscar (2026-10-31): the numbers stand as built and get tuned by playing once dives are playable.
- [x] F2, server-checked fights (done 2026-10-31, branch `f2-server-fights`): the server replays each fight and accepts only a landed fish.
  - `castok` carries the reel seed and difficulty (from `rollCast`). The client steps the reel at a fixed 1/60 s and sends one bit a step with `catch` (`HoldLog`, base64, at most 6,000 characters for the 10-minute fight cap; `maxPayload` is now 8 KB).
  - `land` replays it (`replayFight`) and accepts only a landed fish whose fight fits the time since the bite, taking `clean` from the replay rather than trusting the client. A replay costs ~0.1 ms for a 60 s fight. D2 reads the replay's `wear` in `land`.
  - Play test (Oscar, 2026-10-31, on a `playtest` branch with S1 and D1): no issues catching fish. The pond's tier 4-5 difficulty curve may need retuning, parked in Ideas.
  - Merged with D1, which also touches `reel.ts` and `reelbot.ts`: D1's per-behaviour `this.p` levers sit alongside F2's `lnArith` / `powArith`, and `botFight(d, react, style, rng, brng, tune?, maxT, log?)`.
- [x] F1, fight rework (done 2026-10-31, branch `f1-fight-rework`): started as rule set B, "Line strain", and became a tension-management fight over ten play tests with Oscar. Pond fights too.
  - The rule: keep the gauge green. In a surge (after an orange SURGE! warning) the fish pulls with a set strength, shown as a white marker; below it the fish takes line (TIGHTEN!), and holding pushes tension into the red (EASE OFF!), where a fray mark fills, faster the deeper the red. Two frays snap the line, one per stretch of red; a part-filled mark drains back, and letting go never frays. Fish above the gear also drag line out with the gauge green, less the higher you ride; the fish escapes if it takes all the line. On fish the gear can handle, holding truly green in a surge still reels in. Harder fish pull harder, move the needle faster and surge longer, though a surge builds over at least 0.4 s until far above the gear. The green sweet spot shrinks as a fish outclasses the gear, and "Too strong for your rod!" shows once its pull passes the red. Levers are in `P` (`src/game/reel.ts`).
  - Pond difficulty is a tier ladder (`reelDifficulty`, `progression.ts`): matched gear 1.2 / 1.5 / 1.6 / 1.7 / 1.8 for tiers 1-5, ×1.2 per tier above the rod and ×0.85 per tier below, with species stamina, size (×0.85-1.15), a shiny and the gloves on top. Sea difficulty is left to D1.
  - Also: a +10% clean-catch bonus (`CLEAN_BONUS`; the client sends `clean` with `catch`, trusted until F2), one-time tips for frays (`fray`) and line out (`slack`), the reel's `wear` report (line taken, frays, snapped) for D1's spool, and no `Math.exp`/`Math.sin` in the fight, so F2's server replays match.
  - Tuning: reel bots in `src/game/reelbot.ts` (feathering, ±25% jittered reactions, best of six styles) land half their fish at 2.82 / 2.67 / 2.31 / 1.93 for 0.15 / 0.25 / 0.4 / 0.7 s, within 0.07 of the P1 fit (2.89 / 2.61 / 2.29 / 1.93), so P1's prices and zones stand; fast vs slow +46%. Each play test's change is logged in the Gameplan's "Fight prototype B" tab.
  - Checked: build, tests (375, including the bot fan, the tier ladder, letting go never fraying, drag, surge reeling and exact replays) and `npm run smoke` (74/74), plus headless screenshots of the surge cues and the clean-catch card.
- [x] D5, Shelf and Drop-off species (done 2026-10-30, branch `d5-sea-species`): Pewter Cod, Copper Snapper and the legend Grandmother Grouper (`shelf`); Gale Mackerel, Ironside Tuna and the legend Squall Marlin (`dropoff`), in `FISH_DEFS` with `sea: true` and looks and blurbs in `fishlook.ts`. Still tier 5 for rods and bait, but sea fish sell at base value × 10 (`SEA_VALUE`) instead of tier 5's ×50, so a random catch is about 1,300-1,450 for the Shelf commons and 1,600-1,800 for the Drop-off ones (the legends ~3,900 and ~5,400); they get the plain lake-giant finish with no spirit glow or crown. Reel bots on fight prototype B, jittered ±25% and taking the best of four styles, land half their fish at difficulty 2.89 / 2.61 / 2.29 / 1.93 at 0.15 / 0.25 / 0.4 / 0.7 s reaction. Decided from it (Oscar, 2026-10-30):
  - Difficulty at sea is 1.6 × 1.01^depth × 0.97^rod level × the zone factor, with no clamp. Zones: Shelf 1-15, Drop-off 16-40, Kelp Deep 41-80, Twilight 81-150, Abyss 151+.

- [x] Endgame step 2 of 8 (GAME_DESIGN.md §38.2): the Smokehouse. Built on the `smokehouse` branch over three sessions (v1, R1, R2) and merged into main after R2 for players to test at the pond before charters (Oscar, 2026-10-30)
  - **The rework: three stages** (Oscar, 2026-10-30; full design and the player's path in GAME_DESIGN.md §38.2, charter hooks in §38.1, "Smokehouse hooks"). Oscar's demo found v1's multi-fish racks too slow, and a five-flavour plan with recipe chapters was too complicated, so the Smokehouse gives three buffs, one per stage, each matching what the player is grinding then. **Small Fry** (opens at level 3): tier 1-2 fish give ⭐ XP. **Lake Giants** (first tier 3 catch): tier 3 gives 💰 coins, and dishes open. **Jewels & Spirits** (first tier 4 catch, then tier 5): tiers 4-5 give ✨ shiny chance, in dishes only. The stages are named after their fish because they all open by about level 12; "endgame" means charters. Through the game's phases: in the gear phase (levels 1-15) it gives XP and coins; in the casino phase (15+) ✨ and 💰 speed up the chips, since **buffs apply at catch like the Lucky Charms** (💰 goes into the fish's `mult` in `land`) and spices use up idle coins; in the charter phase (20+, overlapping the casino on purpose) the charter spices help. Stages open on the first catch of the tier (checked from the Fishdex, so existing players get theirs on joining). Dishes cook instantly from smoked fish. **Spices** (coins, optional, one per dish) are the coin sink and, with charters, the charter lever. Smokehouse data never shipped on main, so no save migration. Each R step is its own session on this branch:
    - ✅ **R1, the smoker, snacks and stages** (done 2026-10-31, merged into `smokehouse`). Built as below, plus two changes from Oscar's demo: smoked fish sell **one or all** (Sell 1 sells the most valuable one, since eating takes the cheapest first), and every eat button and the Pantry say what eating does (a snack never gets stronger, each fish adds 5 min up to 30; "Eat 3 → 18 min", "2:41 left → 7:41 after eating one"). Level gifts past 50 stay banked until R2 makes them dishes. Checked: build, tests (329) and a headless-Chrome demo on a scratch save; `npm run smoke` couldn't run cleanly in the cloud container (software WebGL; the unchanged branch failed the same timing checks), so run it locally before merging into main:
      - The smoker: one fish per spot, 5 spots, Smoke all (skips shinies), times by tier (5 s, 10 s, 20 s, 1 min, 2 min), finishing offline too; smoked fish stacked by species in the Pantry.
      - Selling: smoked tier 1-2 fish sell to Mags for 1.5×.
      - Snacks from tiers 1-3: tier 1 +25% XP, tier 2 +50% XP, tier 3 +15% coins. 5 min each, more adds time up to 30, Eat all, one snack at a time.
      - Stage unlocks from the Fishdex with banners; Mags's locked line before level 3, 3 free smoked fish at level 3, the `smoker` and `snack` tips, a `nextGoal` line, and a one-time "New in town" banner for existing players.
      - Buffs applied at catch: ⭐ into the catch XP, 💰 into the fish's `mult` beside the charm (`land`, `src/game/economy.ts`), so they count toward chips too.
      - Catch-card credit ("+120 XP ⭐ Smoked Perch") and HUD chips.
      - Menu tabs: Smoker, Kitchen (locked until the first tier 3 catch), Pantry.
      - Replaces v1's racks, `takeFish`/`unsmoke`, rack loading and its five recipes' buffs (luck, reel control, the dusk bite).
    - ✅ **R2, dishes** (done 2026-10-30). Decided with Oscar while building it: a cooked dish goes into the Pantry to be eaten later (not eaten on cooking), the same dish eaten again adds its time up to an hour, and the `/smokestats` counts are saved with the accounts (`smokestats` beside `accounts` in Postgres, or `smokestats.json`). Pond spices were built (Hickory and Chili, 100 / 500 / 5,000 / 20,000 coins by dish) and then **shelved** in Oscar's demo: the picker only showed the price in small print, and Oscar would rather keep the play test to dishes, so spices come back with charters in R3 (TODO.md, step 4). Built as below:
      - The dish rule: tiers decide what it does (each buff at twice the best snack in it, except XP at most +50%; tier 4 ✨ ×1.5, tier 5 ✨ ×2); fish count decides how long (3 min a fish).
      - Dishes: Fish Cakes, Angler's Supper, Jewel Platter, Spirit Banquet; each unlocks once its top tier (or higher) has been caught.
      - One dish plus one snack; the stronger buff of each kind counts.
      - A free Fish Cakes when the kitchen opens, the `dish` tip naming what to smoke, and dish cards that show the gain against snacking the same fish.
      - Level gifts as the best dish unlocked.
      - A `/smokestats` dev command for the play test: fish smoked and sold by tier, snacks, and dishes cooked and eaten by stage, and the share of online players with a buff.
      - Rules in `src/game/smoke.ts` (`dishBuffs`, `cook`, `eatDish`, `tickBuffs`, `serveGifts`), data in `src/data/smokehouse.ts`, saved as `SaveData.smoke.cooked` and `.dish`; new `cook` and `eatdish` messages; ✨ multiplies the shiny chance in `rollCast`; the catch card credits whichever snack or dish gave each buff; dev `/dish <dish> [N]`; the counters in `server/smokestats.ts`
      - The Kitchen tab lists the four dishes (ready, what's missing, or the catch that unlocks it); the detail shows the buffs, the same fish as snacks, the smoked fish needed as tier pills and the Cook button. Cooked dishes show in the Pantry and the Bag with Eat buttons (asking before replacing a dish with over a minute left), and as a second HUD chip
      - Fixed in Oscar's demo (2026-10-30): a tier 4 or 5 catch didn't open the Kitchen, since stages and dishes checked for a catch of exactly their tier. They now open on that tier or higher (`reachedTier`), so dishes unlock by their top tier, and the `dish` tip shows on any tier 3+ catch that opens the Kitchen
      - Also fixed for the merge: the smoke test's seeded casino player now has the Smokehouse stages seen, since the "New in town" banner's jingle 4.6 s after joining landed mid-spin and failed the roulette pacing check every run

In headless Chrome with a seeded level-22 player: the Kitchen tab, a locked-by-tier dish, cooking an Angler's Supper (the cheapest tier 2 and 3 fish used), the Pantry with dishes, the replace prompt, the HUD chip and a catch card crediting the dish
  - **Opens at level 3** (Oscar, 2026-10-30): Minnow bait brings tier 3 fish at level 4, so opening then would skip the early stage. Early XP snacks are +25%/+50% so they show on a 6 XP catch; dishes' XP stays at +50% (not doubled), since a level gift past 50 is a dish.
  - The v1 plan, now built (2026-10-30): optional, but it becomes what makes deeper charters doable (step 4), so smoked goods are worth having. A smoker NPC and counter; hand fish into a recipe; it smokes on a rack in real time (keeps going offline; 2 racks, a third at level 30) into a Pantry in the save. Every item can be **eaten** at the pond for a 10-minute buff counted in **online time only** (kinds stack, the same kind refreshes, at most 3, trophy-size ingredients +50% time, multiplies with the casino perks, no shiny buff on Shiny Lure casts) or, from step 4, **packed** for a charter. Town recipes: Minnow Bites (6 T1: bites 25% sooner), Kippers (4 T2 incl. a bass: +20% luck), Pike Jerky (3 T3 incl. a pike: +20% reel control), Lantern Glaze (2 Lantern Eels + a 🌙 fish: night bite from dusk, 🌙 fish ×1.5), Jewel Platter (3 T4: shiny ×1.5, level 20). Well-smoked ★: smoking a recipe 10 times doubles its yield. The server applies buffs in `rollCast` and `reelControl`
  - Decided 2026-10-30 when building it: the smoker is **Mags Cinder** (red headscarf, leather apron, a grin; "Fish in, smoke out. Don't touch the racks."), rack times go by tier (5, 10, 15, 20 and 30 min), and the menu is a list with a detail pane where you pick a recipe and fill its empty fish spots. The hotbar holds only 3 fish (5 with the Tackle Vest), so recipes of 4-6 fish couldn't be handed in at once: racks now **load over several trips**, keep the fish handed in, and start smoking once full
  - Changed after Oscar's demo (2026-10-30): the plain counter by the blue cottage up the street (out of the way) became a dark timber **smoke shack** right beside the Lucky Wheel, across the main path from Marlow's stall, with the shore campfire behind it (picked from mockups of several spots; a plain counter got lost behind the wheel everywhere), and the cottage lost its Smokehouse sign. - [x] Endgame step 1 of 8 (plan: GAME_DESIGN.md §38.4): give levels 16-50 a point and remove the level cap. Everything unlocks by level 15 (~3,200 XP), but levels 16-50 take ~66,000 more XP (~1,000 top-gear catches) and give nothing. Decided 2026-10-30: (a) **Titles**: a `titles` list in the save plus one chosen title, shown under the name tag and in the Anglers list, picked in the Wardrobe; charter and Derby titles use it later. (b) **Level ranks** every 5th level, nautical so the order is obvious: 20 Deckhand, 25 Bosun + Oilskin Sou'wester (hat, tier 2), 30 First Mate, 35 Skipper + Storm Slicker (shirt, tier 3), 40 Captain, 45 Commodore + Brass Compass (jewelry, tier 4), 50 Admiral + Admiral's Bicorne (hat, tier 5). The outfits are a new earned-only cosmetic source (`CosShop`, `src/data/cosmetics.ts`), shown locked with their level in the Wardrobe; the gameplay unlocks at 20/30/40 (charters, recipes, a third rack, a third provision slot) are recorded now and take effect when steps 2 and 4 ship. (c) **Progress you can see**: `nextGoal` and `welcomeText` (`src/game/progression.ts:157`) name the next rank and its reward, and a milestone level-up gets a rank banner. (d) **No level cap** (decided 2026-10-30; several players are already level 50): `applyXp` (`progression.ts:44`) throws XP away at `MAX_LEVEL` today. Levels keep going past 50 at a flat `xpForNextLevel(50)` (~3,500 XP, ~50 top-gear catches) each, so the pace never slows; the level pill, its tooltip (`src/game/game.ts:2705`) and `/level` in `server/dev.ts` stop treating 50 as max. Every level past 50 gives a free smoked provision and every 5th a free charter fare (stored until steps 2 and 4 can use them). Admiral stays the last rank; titles and a level-pill colour at 60 Fleet Admiral (bronze), 75 Admiral of the Lakes (silver), 100 Sea Legend (gold), 150 Lord of the Deep (aurora shimmer). **Back pay** for players already at 50, whose XP was thrown away: estimate it from Fishdex counts (each species' count × a typical catch's XP, no shiny or size bonus, minus the ~69,000 XP to reach 50) and pay it out as levels and titles, with the back-paid provisions and fares capped; a dev command previews each account's back pay before it ships. (e) Count shinies per species in `DexEntry` (`src/game/state.ts:6`). Outfit looks (decided 2026-10-30, storm gear, built from primitives in `hatModel` and friends, `src/game/character.ts`): Oilskin Sou'wester (mustard-yellow waxed rain hat, long back brim over the neck, dark chin cord), Storm Slicker (matching yellow rain jacket, hood bunched at the back, wooden toggles, white reflective chest band), Brass Compass (round brass compass on a chain, glowing red needle that slowly swings), Admiral's Bicorne (navy bicorne, gold braid edge, gold rosette with a glowing star, white plume, Legendary sparkles); none of them may look like the stall's Captain's Hat. Rank-up (decided): the big `#banner` ("⚓ BOSUN ⚓" in gold, "Level 25 · Oilskin Sou'wester added to your Wardrobe", the level-up jingle), a server-wide chat line ("⚓ Penny made Bosun!"), and [Wear title] / [Wear it] buttons on the catch card
  - Done (2026-10-30): levels have no cap; from 50 on each costs a flat ~3,500 XP (`FLAT_LEVEL`, `xpForNextLevel`, `src/game/progression.ts`). Ranks, titles and pill tiers live in the new `src/data/ranks.ts`; `grantRanks` gives a rank's title and outfit once (on a catch, in `migrate`, and with dev `/level`), and the four storm-gear outfits are a new `earned` cosmetic source that no counter sells. A catch that reaches a rank sends "⚓ Ann made Bosun!" to the whole server, shows the gold rank banner (it replaces the tier banner on that catch) and puts [Wear title] / [Wear it] on the catch card, which also answer T and O since the mouse is locked while fishing. Titles show under name tags and in the Anglers tab (online and top anglers), are picked in a new Wardrobe Title row with locked ranks greyed, and are checked on the server (`setTitle`, new `title` messages). Levels past 50 bank `gifts` (a provision each, a fare every 5th) for the Smokehouse and charters. Shinies are counted per species (`DexEntry.shiny`)

Back-paid levels give at most 5 provisions and 1 fare

The wider "one bad message takes the server down" gap is a new item in Ideas

- [x] 💬 Make the day/night cycle follow the server's clock, not each device's clock: players reported some saw night while others saw day at the same moment
  - Likely cause: each client draws the sky from its own device clock (`dayFraction(Date.now() / 1000)` in `World.updateDaylight`, `src/game/world.ts:390`), and a day lasts only 10 minutes (`DAY_SECONDS`, `src/game/daynight.ts`), so a phone clock off by a minute or two shifts its whole cycle. The server already decides night bites from its own clock (`server/room.ts:493`), so a skewed client can also show night while the server rolls day fish. Fix: the server sends its time (or the day fraction) on join and now and then, and the client uses that offset instead of its own clock
  - Also check the dev `/time` override (`m.dayFrac` in `server/room.ts`, `dayOverride` set in `src/game/game.ts:285`) only affects the player who typed it, and that it stays obvious when it's on
  - Done (2026-10-30): the server sends its clock (`now`, ms) in `welcome` and again in a `clock` message every minute (`CLOCK_MS`, from `Room.snapshot`), in case a device's clock jumps mid-session. `NetClient` keeps the difference from the device's clock and `net.now()` gives the server's time; `World.clock` (Date.now by default, so offline play still follows the device) points at it, and `updateDaylight` reads it. Message delay is ignored, since it's well under a second and a day is 10 minutes. Night bites already used the server's clock, so the sky and the server now agree
  - `/time` was already per player: the override lives on the member (`m.dayFrac`) and the reply only goes to the player who typed it (new test assertion). Reconnecting always reloads the page, so the client's override can't outlive the server's. To keep it obvious, the night chip now says "🕒 /time is on (only you) · /time auto to undo" while it's set, next to the night line if it's dark. It's keyed on the `/time` reply, not `world.dayOverride`, so the screenshot and bench scripts' `day()` hook doesn't put the chip in README shots
  - Checked: headless Chrome with two players, one whose `Date.now` ran 5 minutes (half a day) fast: its sky clock matched the server's to within 15 ms and both showed the same night. New room test for the `welcome` and periodic `clock` times. Build, tests (275) and smoke (74/74) pass
- [x] Hitching for the first few seconds after load, very noticeable on phones. Likely shader programs compiling on first draw rather than textures (most textures are generated in code); `renderer.compileAsync(scene, camera)` behind the join screen could hide it. Measure before fixing
  - Measured (2026-10-30) with a new probe, `npm run bench:load` (`scripts/hitch.mjs`): a fresh browser per run, so no cached shaders. It times every frame and the blocking GL calls from load through the join screen, joining, standing 3 s, turning a full circle and standing 3 s. Shaders were the cause: texture and buffer uploads were under 20 ms in total, and no map building or GC showed up after the first frame. On the M1 the first frame behind the join screen compiled 26 programs (~150-200 ms); after joining, the first look around compiled 13 more (things out of view at the spawn), plus 3 for the first other player's avatar online. On the phone stand-in (SwiftShader at 1400x647, as a touch device tapping "Try touch controls") those post-join programs cost 2.6-3.0 s of main thread and froze frames for up to 1.5-2.2 s, with more time in the GPU process on each object's first draw. Two stalls that aren't shaders: making the `AudioContext` on the first click took ~200 ms in headless Chrome (now in Ideas), and an idle gap of ~500 ms after the first frame in headless Chrome with no work on any thread
  - Fix: `World.prewarm` runs `renderer.compileAsync(scene, camera)` after each map build (pond, casino) and when lite/full graphics changes (shadows change most programs). It compiles every material in the scene, hidden ones included, with the real lights (the LightPool's fixed 8), fog and shadows, so day and night share programs. While the join screen is up, `Game` sets `world.holdForShaders` and the world isn't drawn until the compile is done, so the join screen doesn't freeze on a half-done compile (the world shows through the 88% backdrop a moment later; the join screen itself appears as before). Once it's done, `warmDraw` draws everything once, hidden objects included and nothing culled, clipped to one pixel, plus a shadow pass: some GPUs (SwiftShader, likely phones) only finish a program on its first draw, and the compile alone left the phone stand-in's post-join stalls in place. `revealAll` (`src/game/prewarm.ts`, tested) keeps lights as they were, since a different light count would compile unused programs. `Avatars` keeps a hidden stand-in avatar in the scene, so other players' shaders are compiled before anyone appears. Map changes during play don't hold the view; they compile in the background, then do the warm-up draw
  - Before/after (alternating runs of main and this branch with the same probe; Metal 3 pairs (night 2), SwiftShader 2): M1 offline, after joining: 13 programs and 120-141 ms of compiling → none, worst frames besides the click stall 33 ms → 17 ms. M1 online: 16 programs (145-155 ms) → none. Night: 13 → none. Phone stand-in after joining: 8-13 programs and 2.6-3.0 s of compiling → none, worst frames 1.5 s / 2.2 s → 0.32 s / 0.35 s (SwiftShader's normal frames are 0.1-0.3 s). The trade-off is all on the join screen: on the M1 one ~320 ms stall at ~0.6 s instead of ~150 ms (42 programs including 5 shadow ones), on the phone stand-in 5.9-6.6 s of compiling there instead of 2.7-3.2 s (worst gaps 4.9-5.4 s against 4.4-4.9 s). The total compile time is about the same; it moved from after joining to before. Repeat runs agreed within ~15% on a quiet machine; a third round under heavy load from other sessions (load average 35-43) was noisier and is left out for the phone. Checked: `npm run build`, `npm test` (239/239), `npm run smoke` 68/68
- [x] 🎨💬 Casino scoreboard for the biggest win and biggest loss in a single hand or spin (from `/idea`, Oscar C, 2026-10-29), maybe a board on the casino wall like Top Anglers (`buildLeaderboard`, `src/game/hub.ts:384`). Decide whether it's all-time or daily, and record results on the server where hands settle (`Room.bjAct`, `server/room.ts`), not from the client. Pairs well with the hand-history item below
  - Done (2026-10-30): Oscar chose all-time only: one biggest single win and one biggest single loss, kept for good like Top Anglers. Every gamble counts (blackjack hands, roulette spins and the outdoor Lucky Wheel), and the board says which game each record came from (a default, easy to narrow to the casino tables). Amounts are coins as players see them (`netWin`, `src/game/casino.ts`): the fish's rounded value after less before (Lucky Charms are already in the fish's `mult`, so `fishValue` matches the hotbar), less any double-down stake that didn't come back. So a plain win nets the fish's value, a natural ×3 twice it, a doubled win (fish ×4, the stake of about the fish's value spent) about twice it, a loss the fish's value, a doubled loss the fish plus the stake, and a push nothing. Recorded on the server only, where results are applied: `Room.bjDone` (`settleBlackjack` now returns the hand's net), and the roulette and wheel spins' `run` closures, so a spin counts when it lands, not when it's rolled. Each account keeps its own biggest win and loss (`Account.casino`: amount, game, fish id, shiny, date), saved beside `supporter` outside the save, so a progress reset keeps it; `AccountStore.records()` takes the biggest across every account, online or not (the earlier one keeps a tie), cached like the hall of fame. Deleting a password-free account drops its records and the next biggest moves up, broadcast like the supporters list. Clients get a `records` message on join and, from every room, whenever a record falls. The board (`RecordsBoard`, `src/game/casinorecords.ts`, spot `casino.records` in `src/data/maps.ts`) hangs on the back wall between the blackjack-side painting and the corner (x 14.4), clear of the pillars' sightline from the entrance, the paintings, tables and paths: a gold frame on dark wood with a gold cap, baked into one mesh, plus one canvas plane redrawn only when the records change (2 draw calls). It shows ▲ BIGGEST WIN / ▼ BIGGEST LOSS, each with the date, the name (shrunk to fit), the amount (+gold / −red), and the fish (✨, tier, name in its tier colour) · game, or "No record yet". No chat or toast on a new record (spoiler risk with a paced blackjack reveal); that's an idea in TODO. New tests in `server/casino.test.ts` (sent on join; roulette win applied at the end of the spin and broadcast; green ×16 and a roulette loss; the wheel; a doubled win nets 2,000 on a 1,000 fish and a doubled bust costs 8,000; a natural and a plain win, a push ignored; smaller and tied results don't replace the record or broadcast; reload and reset keep it) and `server/accounts.test.ts` (deleting the holder hands the record to the next best). Smoke checks the board is in the scene and that the first settled roulette spin goes up on it. Checked in headless Chrome with seeded records and with none (close up, from the entrance and from the blackjack table). The casino poster (`npm run screenshots -- casino`) now shows the board small on the back wall, left of the blackjack-side painting; not re-shot in this change. Build, tests (245) and smoke (69/69) pass
  - Merged after the paced casino reveals (2026-10-30): the server sends `records` as a bet settles, which can be before your own reveal ends, so `onRecords` skips the board while a reveal is running and `endReveal` puts it up with the result, like the held save. New smoke check: the board changes only after the result (8 ms after on an M1). Smoke 74/74
- [x] 🎨💬 Make gambling feel more suspenseful; today it "feels a bit disconnected" (from `/idea`, Oscar C, 2026-10-29). Taste-driven: first ask which games and moments feel flat, then propose options (e.g. a slower reveal, a short drumroll before the result, camera or sound cues on the big moment)
  - Done (2026-10-30): Oscar said all three games felt about equally flat and chose ticking spins with a beat before the result, and a paced blackjack deal (not a camera push-in or stakes-scaled effects). The server still decides everything when it did; only the client's reveal changed. Pacing numbers live in `src/game/tablepace.ts` (tested in `tablepace.test.ts`)
  - Lucky Wheel and roulette: each peg (the Lucky Wheel now has gold pegs painted on its wedge edges) or pocket edge passing the pointer plays a short knock (`audio.peg`: woodier and louder for the Lucky Wheel, lighter and quieter for roulette), worked out from the wheel's real angle each frame, so the ticks follow its speed. Several in one frame are spread across it (at most 4), and a fast rattle is softer, so the last slow clicks stand out. Both wheels now slow at a steady rate (like friction) instead of the cubic ease-out, so they come to a clear stop instead of creeping. Then 450 ms of quiet (`RESULT_BEAT_MS`), then the banner and sound. Spin lengths are unchanged (3.8 s and 4.2 s); results now land at 4.25 s and 4.65 s. Measured in headless Chrome: Lucky Wheel 52 ticks with the gaps growing from about 35 ms to 233 ms, the last one 0.46 s before the stop; roulette about 200 ticks, the last gap 150 ms, the last 0.19 s before the stop
  - Blackjack: a card shoe at the dealer's left, and each card slides from it in a little arc (300 ms) with a soft card sound (`audio.card`). The opening deal goes player, dealer, player, then the dealer's card face down, 120 ms apart (the last lands at about 1.6 s). Hit and double cards deal the same way. Once the hand is decided the hole card turns over (350 ms, a half turn on its long side): straight away on a stand, or after a 550 ms hold following a natural, a bust or a double. Each dealer draw comes after a 600 ms hold. The result banner and sound wait 450 ms after the last card, including a natural on the deal (result at about 2.9 s). The table readout counts only the cards down so far ("Dealer ?" until the hole card is turned), and the buttons are hidden until the cards have landed, so clicks during the deal do nothing. With `prefers-reduced-motion` cards appear in place when they would have landed and the hole card swaps face at once, on the same timings. A last pass puts every card in place if frames stopped mid-deal (a hidden tab)
  - No early spoilers: while a reveal runs, the server's new save is held back and applied with the result, so the hotbar fish value or empty slot, the coins, the table readout and any open menu only change then. The wheel and the roulette table already settled as the spin ended; for blackjack the server now sends the finished hand before the save that pays it (a small reorder in `Room.bj`/`Room.bjAct`, nothing else changed), so the client knows to hold the save. Other actions (slots, drop, sell, bets, cash-in) stay locked until the result as during a spin, and the casino exit says "Wait for the result first." Other players never saw your wheels or cards, so there's nothing for them to pace; your carried fish still vanishes for them when the server settles, as before. The trailer script's result mark includes the beat
  - Checked: tests (the pacing plans, tick spacing, and a server test that the finished hand goes out before the save), build, and smoke 71/71 with new checks: roulette ticks slow down and there's a beat before the result, then the hotbar; blackjack deals one card at a time with no buttons until the last; the result waits for the last card and the hotbar for the result. A headless Chrome run against a seeded local server recorded the sounds, server messages, banners and hotbar changes for a wheel spin, a roulette spin, a natural on the deal and a stand with three dealer draws, with screenshots mid-deal
- [x] 🎨📋💬 Dedicated rod and bait slots in the hotbar: same total number of slots, but two are always the rod and the bait, so fish can't crowd them out (from `/idea`, Oscar C, 2026-10-29). Today every slot holds a rod, a fish or a bait stack (`src/game/state.ts:10-15`, `renderHotbar`, `src/game/game.ts:2623`). Needs decisions first: how the two slots look and where they sit, what happens to a save holding several rods or bait types in the hotbar (move extras to the tackle locker?), and how scroll/number-key selection and the active bait (`eco.activeSlot`) work with them
  - Done (2026-10-30): Oscar chose the layout: slot 1 (key 1) is always the rod and slot 2 (key 2) the bait, with a teal frame, a ROD / BAIT tag under each and a small gap before the fish; slots 3 and up hold fish only (3-5, 3-7 with the Tackle Vest), same total. An empty gear slot shows a faded rod or worm. Rods and bait are swapped only in the Bag; number keys and scroll just move between slots, and selecting slot 1 or 2 still sets the active rod / bait. The slot array keeps its shape (`ROD_SLOT`, `BAIT_SLOT`, `FISH_SLOT` in `state.ts`), so the save and network formats are unchanged
  - Rules (`economy.ts`): only fish slots count as free (`freeSlots`), so a full fish row means a refused cast ("Your fish slots are full - sell or drop a fish first.") or a catch that gets away, and the last bait no longer counts as freeing a slot. Buying, digging, `/rod` and `/bait` go to the locker and equip into the gear slot as before, which never needs a free slot now. Picking up a rod puts it in the locker (into the rod slot if empty) and is refused for a rod you already own; picked-up bait tops up the bait slot if it's empty or the same type, the rest goes to the locker (refused past the 200 locker cap). Dropping your rod (G) brings out your best other rod, and your only rod still can't be dropped; a dropped bait stack refills from the locker like a used-up one, and running out leaves the slot empty. "Put away in locker" (`stowSlot`, the `stow` message) is gone, since stowing gear no longer frees anything for fish; the server ignores `stow` from old clients
  - Old saves (`layoutSlots` in `migrate`, runs on every load and leaves a save that already fits unchanged): the equipped rod goes in slot 1 (else any carried rod, else one from the locker) and the active bait in slot 2 (merged across stacks up to 99; else the first carried stack; else a stack from the locker). Every other rod and bait stack goes to the locker, with nothing lost (the locker may go past 200 here). Fish already in slots 3+ stay put; fish from slots 1-2 fill free fish slots. Fish that still don't fit wait in extra slots past the end of the bar (dashed outline, tooltip "extra slot until it is sold") instead of being cashed out, and each extra slot goes away once its fish is sold, dropped, lost at the wheel or tables, or turned in (`clearSlot`); buying the vest turns them into ordinary slots. The held item stays in hand. The very old layouts (bait counts, `ownedRods`) now go through the same path instead of cashing out or dropping what didn't fit
  - Checked: new unit tests for the migration (unchanged save, several rods and bait in the hotbar, merged stacks, gear only in the locker, fish overflow on a 5 and a vest-sized bar), gear swaps with a full fish row, pickups, drops, catching and the casino with extra slots, plus Room tests (refused cast on a full row even on the last bait, a catch lost when the row fills mid-fight, a duplicate rod left on the ground, `stow` ignored). Headless Chrome against a seeded server: a fresh save, an old save with two rods and two bait types in the hotbar (equipped ones kept, extras in the locker, Bag swaps survive a reload), an old save with four fish and no bait (one extra slot), the full-row cast refusal, keys and scroll, and touch mode (taps select the gear and fish slots). New smoke check that slots 1 and 2 carry the tags; smoke 69/69. README `reel` and `wardrobe` screenshots re-shot (they show the hotbar). The trailer's casino accounts now have the vest and bet from keys 3-6 (trailer not re-recorded). Build and tests pass (248)
- [x] 🎨 Profanity filter for player names. Chat was already filtered (`censor` in `server/chat.ts` masks rude words, and feedback uses it too); names weren't, on join or (once it exists) on rename
  - Done (2026-10-30): Oscar chose names only (chat masking unchanged), refusing a rude name when a new account claims it, with the chat word list matched more strictly. `rudeName(name)` in `server/chat.ts` (exported so the rename feature can reuse it) applies the chat rules (`ROOTS` anywhere, `WORDS` as whole words, leetspeak undone, repeats squeezed) to each piece of the name, split at spaces, symbols, underscores and hyphens, then again at capitals ("BigDickFisher", "XXAss") and at digits and leet symbols ("Ass99", "2Dick4U"); spaced-out single letters are joined ("f u c k", "F_U_C_K"), and it checks the name both as typed and with symbols dropped ("Fu.ck" saves as "Fuck"). Whole-word ones still need a whole piece, so "Cassandra", "Dickens", "Classic", "Grassy", "Hancock" and "Peacock" pass while "HanCock" doesn't. A names-only safe list (`NAME_SAFE`) lets real words that contain a root through: "Scunthorpe", "Yoshitaka"/"Toshitaka", "Shiitake" and "FishIt", unless a capital splits the root out ("FiShit", "YoShit"). `AccountStore.auth` checks only when a new account claims a name (both the name as sent and the saved 16-character cut, which could end in "…Ass"), and refuses with `The name "BigDickFisher" isn't allowed. Please pick another.`; an account that already has such a name keeps logging in, and a password `login` for a new name goes through the same check. The client needed no change: join errors already show in red under the name box (`joinMessage`) and leave the form ready for another try. Tests in `chat.test.ts` (21 refused, 24 allowed names) and `accounts.test.ts` (refused claims, nothing created, an existing "Ass99" still logs in and its name stays taken). Checked in headless Chrome against a local server: "BigDickFisher" shows the message and stays on the join screen, "Cassandra" then joins from the same screen, and a seeded "Ass99" account joins with its token. Build and tests (239) pass. Smoke not run. Known gaps (all-lowercase or all-caps compounds like "bigdickfisher") and chat still masking the safe-list words are in Ideas

At full night in `World.updateDaylight`: hemisphere light 0.34 → 0.56 (sky #6a80c0 → #7088cc, ground #141c30 → #1d2a48), moon 0.45 → 0.72 (#7f9bff → #84a0ff) and raised from 0.06 to 0.13 elevation so it reaches flat ground, sky horizon/fog #0c1730 → #152649 and top #02040c → #050a19, water darkening 0.78/0.7 → 0.75/0.67, mountains 0.82 → 0.75, clouds #1c2540 → #26304f. Exposure, lamps, windows and the night glow are unchanged, so lamps still carry the scene. On the dark beach, sand luma goes 12 → 23 and water 32 → 40 (of 255). Every change is weighted by nightness, so the day look is unchanged (day renders at old and new values match). Checked with headless Chrome renders of all four angles at night and in the day, plus `npm run build` and `npm test`. Smoke not run

The form is a Bug / Idea toggle, a text box (500 characters, the server's limit; Enter sends, Shift+Enter is a new line) and Send / Cancel. It sends the same `/bug` or `/idea` command as chat, so the server's rate limits, censoring and Discord posting are reused; the server's `dev` reply now carries `sent: true/false`, and the form shows that reply in green or red ("Thanks! Your bug report was sent…", the rate-limit line) instead of chat, while `/bug` typed in chat still answers in chat. Offline it says feedback needs a connection; no answer in 8 s or a dropped connection lets the player try again. Typing doesn't trigger keybinds (Tab no longer closes the menu from a menu text box), Esc closes like other menus and the draft is kept. Form logic in `src/game/feedback.ts` with tests; server tests check the `sent` flag and that the form's limits match the server's. Checked in headless Chrome against a local server with the webhooks unset (pause button, focus, keys while typing, Esc and reopen, too short, sent, rate limit, offline, chat `/bug` unchanged). New smoke check (pause button → idea sent → thanked in the form), and smoke now blanks the Discord webhooks for its server; smoke not run

Ideas: briefly show a new line at full opacity or with a highlight/slide-in that fades back, maybe a soft sound for messages from other players. Taste-driven: propose options first

A new line shows at full strength with a soft gold edge for 3 s, then eases back to the faded look over 1 s (`FLASH_MS`, `.chat-line.fresh`). Several lines arriving together each flash; while typing everything stays full as before. With `prefers-reduced-motion` a line just holds at full strength for 2.5 s and snaps back. The 50% fade moved from the `#chat` window onto each line and the hint (`--fade`), since a child can't be brighter than its parent; the faded and typing looks are unchanged (server notices still 0.375/0.75). The window is rebuilt on every change, so a flashing line gets a negative `animation-delay` and carries on rather than restarting. Smoke: the faded/typing check now reads a line (`{"faded":0.5,"full":1}`) and a new check sees two lines from another player at full strength that fade back after about 4 s (`backMs` 4052). - [x] 🎨💬 NPCs wander too far and end up on top of players, "kind of jump scares" (from `/idea`, Oscar C, 2026-10-29). Probably the walk-on rule in `NpcWalker` (`src/game/npcwalk.ts`): after 10 s beside a player who stays put they walk on, and since NPCs are walk-through their route can pass straight through the player (e.g. Old Salty on the pier). Options: shorter routes, re-route or wait instead of passing within ~1.5 m of a player, or keep them stopped while someone is fishing nearby
  - Done (2026-10-30): Oscar chose wait, then reroute. Walking townsfolk never pass within 1.5 m of a player (`NPC_CLEARANCE`): each frame they look 2 m along their leg (clearance + look-ahead = the 3.5 m chat range, so you still get the stop to chat first) and stand still if anyone ahead is that close to it. After 2 s (`NPC_GIVE_WAY`) they turn back and walk their loop the other way round, using the same legs reversed so walks still stay clear of water and props; if the way back is blocked too they just wait, so they never flip back and forth. While anyone within 3.5 m is fishing (casting through to looking at the catch: local phase not idle, or another player's `cast`/`hold` act) they stay put, plus 4 s after (`NPC_FISH_LINGER`) to cover the gap between casts. Stop-to-chat is unchanged and still only for you. NPC motion is per-client, so `Game.syncBystanders` passes the local player plus every other player on the map (from `net.players`) through `World` and `Town` to `NpcWalker.update`; the casino staff use the same rules. New tests in `npcwalk.test.ts` (clearance with players on, beside and at stops, you or others; chat first; give way then turn back; walks past someone 1.8 m off; stays put while you or someone else fishes; no dithering when boxed in or pacing round someone). A throwaway scan of every real walk with a player stood at ~180 spots never came closer than 1.5 m, and the only NPCs left standing were at home with the player on their one way out (Old Salty, Captain Reed). Build and tests pass; smoke not run

"Play offline" on the join screen shows nothing. It sits outside `#hud` above the resume overlay, so the button can be clicked whenever the mouse is free. A reload rejoins by itself for returning players (`fishing-auto`). - [x] Light the distant town and dock at night without adding real lights
  - Only the 8 point lights nearest the camera are on (each one costs every lit pixel), so far lamps looked switched off and the pier vanished at night. Options at four levels (2026-10-22): https://claude.ai/artifact/CSfsXXhLZuo8NxUcF6yqCS. Oscar picked level 3 after trying it locally
  - Done (2026-10-22): `src/game/nightglow.ts` adds soft halos round bulbs, lanterns, fires, torches and windows; light pools on the ground and dock under lamps whose real light is off; and shimmering lamp reflections on the lake. One draw call each, hidden by day. Glow sources are tagged where they're built (`glowTag`)
  - Pools use three.js's own point-light falloff and are calibrated per surface to about 0.7-0.8x the real light (measured on the same patch of ground). Pools add light while real lights scale with the surface colour, so the dark dock planks need a much lower gain (`POOL_GAIN`)
  - `LightPool` fades lights over 0.4 s (`LIGHT_FADE`) instead of popping them on: a light leaving the nearest 8 dims to zero, then hands its slot to the incoming one, so the light count (and shaders) never change. Each pool crossfades with its lamp's real light. Tests in `lightpool.test.ts`
  - Cost (`bench:ab`, 6 alternating pairs on an M1 against main): plazaNight +2% (about 0.04 ms at 2.3 ms, 5 of 6 pairs slower), pondNight and every day scene unchanged (within noise), draw calls +3 at night. Map build and first-render timings were noisy in both directions. Results in `bench-results/ab-main-vs-943a328.json`. Smoke 58/58; README night screenshot re-shot
- [x] Visual polish for the outdoor world, keeping the cute low-poly look

  - Textures drawn in code (`src/game/textures.ts`), multiplied over existing colours: ground dabs, dock plank grain, cottage siding and shingle roofs, stone well and chimneys, two-tone rocks
  - Scenery: two rings of snow-capped mountains tinted by the sky, about a quarter of the trees round and leafy, grass tufts and flower clusters on grassy ground only (off sand, paths, hidden bottles and the worm patch), lily pads in the shallows. Lite graphics hides the tufts and flowers
  - Done (2026-10-22): render time +2.3-2.6% in the pond and town scenes (about 0.05 ms a frame at 2.2 ms), casino unchanged (+0.2%, the control). Three alternating runs each against main on a quiet machine. Triangles 88k to 131k (pond) and draw calls +3 (pond) / +18 (town, the textured cottages only merge with same-coloured parts). Mountains went from 18 meshes to 4. No leaked geometries or textures across map changes. Grass blades are open-ended cones and flowers octahedra to halve their triangles. `npm run bench` works again (`openMenu('wardrobe')`, and `BENCH_PORT` overrides 5299). Smoke 57/57; README screenshots regenerated (fish lineup unchanged)

Hard rule: a tip gives nothing in game (no coins, chips, perks or head start), because selling anything that feeds the casino is real money for gambling currency

- [x] Phones: white screen after joining, and the styling changes slowed them down
  - A friend on Android (Brave, touch controls on) saw a white screen with the HUD and a small icon top left after creating a character: Chromium's "sad canvas", drawn when the GPU process crashes. Couldn't reproduce on desktop GPUs; every shader is far inside phone uniform/texture limits, so the likely cause is load or GPU memory right after joining
  - The outdoor painted textures, casino style pass and night glow cost about +3% on an M1 but +20-27% outdoors on SwiftShader at the touch resolution (a rough stand-in for a fill-rate-bound phone); the grass tufts and flowers alone were about 15-20%
  - Done (2026-10-22): touch devices start in Lite graphics, and ⚙️ Settings has a Full/Lite switch (remembered per browser; the dev FPS meter uses the same setting). Lite now frees the 2048² shadow map, the hidden character preview shrinks its drawing buffer to 1x1, and touch mode drops the join screen's backdrop blur. A lost WebGL context shows a "Graphics stopped working" banner with Reload, and the reload starts in Lite. Lite renders in about half the time of Full on SwiftShader (pond 205 → 96 ms, town square ~235 → ~125 ms), below what phones paid before the styling changes. Build, 208 unit tests and a headless phone run (lite on after join, toggle, preview buffer, simulated context loss) pass. `npm run smoke` 62/63: the one failure, "pond night: town lamps lit", fails on `main` too. Not yet checked on a real phone

Chromium had most likely blocked WebGL for the site since his crash (on Android until the browser restarts), so `new Game()` threw and `main.ts` never wired up "Try touch controls". Reproduced with `--disable-3d-apis`. Now a failed start shows why on the notice panel (close the browser fully and come back) with Reload, phones start in Lite from page load, and nothing is drawn behind the notice until the player taps through. New smoke check for the no-WebGL page (66/66)

  - Done (2026-10-21): `openMenu` frees the mouse with `exitPointerLock()`, so Chrome hands it straight back to `closeMenu` without a click, and the same Esc then releases it again. A lock loss within 1 s of an Esc close now just shows "Click to keep playing" instead of the pause menu. Smoke check added; not yet tried by hand in a real browser

Hard parts: iPhone Safari has no Pointer Lock (game activity is tied to it in `game.ts`), input handling is mixed into `game.ts`, menus rely on hover, and phone GPUs vs the current shadows/lights
  - [x] What would need to change?

- [x] Real accounts with a password, so players can log in from another browser or device

Add an optional "set a password" step that claims the name; password-free play stays as it is, and existing accounts keep working
  - Plan agreed (2026-10-21): the browser token stays the everyday login; a password only gets a new browser in, and that browser's token is then added to the account (`tokens: string[]` replaces `hash`, about 10 devices max; old saves load as `tokens: [hash]`, and the server keeps writing `hash` too, so a rollback to the current server keeps every account). Async `crypto.scrypt` for passwords, lockout per name plus server-wide, pulled out of `DevGate` (`server/dev.ts`) so both share it. The join handler in `server/index.ts` becomes async: guard against a second `join` while one is checking, and re-check "online" after the await
  - Protocol: optional `pass` on `join`, `error` with `need: 'password'` for a taken name that has one, new `setpass { pass, old? }` (changing it signs out other devices), `hasPass` in `welcome`. UI: password field on the join screen only when the server asks for it; Account section in the ⚙️ settings tab (set/change password, log out of this device, warning if no password)
  - Decisions: password optional, with a nudge on the welcome-back screen from about level 3; recovery = any still-logged-in device can set a new password without the old one, otherwise Oscar resets it with a dev command (`/resetpass <name>`), no email; newest login wins (the older session is disconnected with "You logged in somewhere else")
  - Tests: new `server/accounts.test.ts` (token login, password adds a device, wrong password, lockout, device cap, old-save migration, setpass), then `npm run smoke`
  - Done (2026-10-21) as planned, with two changes: `setpass` takes no `old` password (any logged-in browser can set a new one, per the recovery decision), and "Log out of this device" only appears once a password is set instead of warning. `/resetpass <name> <new password>` takes the new password, so Oscar can hand it to the player. Verified with 11 unit tests, a headless-Chrome run of the whole flow (set password, second browser asked for it, wrong/right password, older tab disconnected, reload remembered, logout, level-3 nudge) and `npm run smoke` (52/52)
- [x] Game world sometimes renders smaller than the screen, with black bars below and to the side
  - Seen on a fresh load or refresh, mostly on mobile; a friend saw it on desktop too. The HUD fills the screen but the 3D view doesn't, so the canvas is stuck at a stale size. `World.resize()` (`src/game/world.ts:234`) sets the canvas to `innerWidth`/`innerHeight` (fixed px, as inline style) only at startup and on `window` `resize`, so a viewport that settles later without a `resize` event (mobile URL bar, touch mode swapping the viewport meta to `width=1400` at `src/game/game.ts:780`, page zoom) leaves it wrong
  - Likely fix: size from the canvas's own CSS box (CSS `width/height: 100%`, a `ResizeObserver` like the character preview at `src/game/character.ts:613`, or a check each frame) and also listen to `visualViewport` resize. Try to reproduce first (refresh in phone emulation and on a real phone)
  - Done (2026-10-22): the canvas is sized by CSS (`#game` 100% x 100%) and `World.resize()` follows its box with a `ResizeObserver`, calling `setSize(w, h, false)` so no stale inline px sizes are left; the pixel ratio is recomputed there too (zoom, other screens). `visualViewport` wasn't needed, since the canvas fills the layout viewport. Reproduced in headless Chrome by swallowing `resize` events and changing the viewport (`main`: canvas stuck at 1100x700 in a 1600x900 window; fixed: fills it), and checked a phone in landscape with `?touch`. Not yet checked on a real phone. Build, 202 unit tests and `npm run smoke` (57/57) pass

Arriving from the casino should place the player just inside the pond `gate` (`maps.ts:99`, `x: 0, z: 56`) facing into town

It applies whenever you arrive from the map the gate leads to (gate travel, and a dev `/tp` from the casino); logins and other arrivals keep the dock spawn. The spot is on the open street, ~3 m short of the gate's "Travel" prompt so it doesn't pop up on arrival. Unit tests, plus a smoke check (58/58) that the real exit lands there

- [x] In-game dev console for debugging/testing
  - Locked behind a username/password, with the check done on the server (not just the client) so regular players can't unlock it; ideally slash commands typed into the existing chat (`src/game/chatbox.ts`), e.g. give money/bait/fish, set level, teleport, set time of day
  - Larger item: propose a plan (auth approach, command list, where commands run) before editing
- [x] New-player guidance through the core loop
  - Contextual tips that walk a new player through catch first fish → sell it at the shop → buy better bait → catch better fish, triggered by milestones (first spawn, first catch, first sale, first bait purchase) rather than a wall of text
  - Should feel natural in the existing UI (hint lines, Old Salty, the welcome screen), show once per account, and stay out of the way. Taste-driven: propose options before coding
- [x] Safety net for players with no bait and no money: dig for worms on the beach
  - Only players with no bait anywhere (hotbar + locker) who can't afford a worm pack can dig; everyone else never sees a prompt, marker or hint
  - A small, plain dirt mound on the sand left of the pier, visible from the dock. When the player can dig, their own client shows wriggling worms and a bobbing marker over it
  - E digs up one pack of worms (5), auto-equipped. Server checks distance and eligibility, plus a 30 s per-account cooldown so drop-and-redig can't farm worms
  - Hints point to the spot when eligible: the out-of-bait cast message, the inventory hint, the shop, and a line from Old Salty
- [x] Make the wardrobe/cosmetics shop player preview proportionally sized
  - The spinning character preview is too small; size it in proportion to the menu panel instead of fixed pixels

- [x] Add an in game element by the casino teleport that add a popup with a screenshot of the inside of the casino
  - This is nice to show newer players whats waiting for them.

  - Higher tiers of fish should be more extreme/special (visually and in size)
- [x] Join/welcome-back screen
  - Currently joining just shows the inventory; replace it with a proper join/welcome-back screen. Taste-driven: propose options before coding
- [x] Cosmetic rework
  - Change the cosmetics' appearance so they fit the game's theme better. Taste-driven: propose options before coding
- [x] UI sometimes disappears until Escape/Tab is toggled
  - Find the repro first; the UI only comes back after pressing/toggling Escape or Tab
- [x] Smaller bait purchase popup, and reconsider the 20-bait cap
  - The shop's purchase popup is too large and blocks the view; it should just say the amount purchased
  - Oscar is fine with holding more than 20 bait unless there's a reason for the limit (`BAIT_STACK` in `src/game/state.ts:11`, covered by `economy.test.ts`)
- [x] Inventory/wardrobe shows equipped charms and their total buffs
  - List the equipped charms and the summed buffs they give
- [x] Show a prompt in the casino when the player can trade fish for chips
  - A UI hint appears when the player is in range and able to click to trade fish in for chips
- [x] Hotbar fish icons show only total value
  - Drop the "x.x" multiplier text on hotbar fish slots and show just the total value
- [x] Casino game menus/pop-ups block the screen since the UI changes
  - Regression from the menu/HUD polish; casino game panels should stay out of the way of the view
- [x] Rework the fish and tiers into a cohesive set
  - Each tier gets a clear identity (Shallows, Sport fish, Lake giants, Jewel fish, Spirit fish) carried through look, name and value
  - Distinct 3D silhouettes and species markings instead of one shared egg body; tier 5 stays recognisably fish; 2D icons match
  - Balance pass: better gear catches fewer low-tier fish, values spread sensibly, bait cost vs earnings checked, catch mixes covered by tests
  - Fishdex and catch card: one-line description, day/night note
  - Keep all fish ids so saves, Fishdex progress and records carry over; show renames before applying
- [x] Lower the global chat's opacity when not in chat mode
  - Fade the chat window while the player isn't typing; return to full opacity when they press Enter to open chat mode
- [x] Show a "XXXX has joined" message in chat when a player joins
  - Sent to everyone already on the server, using the joining player's name
- [x] Escape always closes the current menu
  - If any menu is open (inventory, shop, wardrobe, etc.), pressing Escape closes it
- [x] Polish the menu systems
  - Inventory and shop menus are clumsy and cluttered; improve the flow so it's clear what everything does
- [x] General visual cleanup
  - The HUD shows a lot of unnecessary information; tone it down and remove unneeded elements
  - The 3D world is fine as is
- [x] Faces on player models
  - Simple, happy, fun faces
  - Painted texture on the head sphere, on the side the player is looking
  - Optionally A/B against a simple 3D face if it fits the theme better and doesn't cost much performance
  - Random face assigned on account creation; pickable in the wardrobe
  - Existing accounts without a face get a random one at login
- [x] Remove the bench blocking the path in front of the well in the town square
  - Well benches are placed by `Town.benches()` (`src/game/town.ts:163`). Remove only the one in front of the well and keep the others
- [x] Remove the Town Fund from the game entirely
  - The level-15 casino lock (`CASINO_LEVEL`) is the only gate needed
  - Remove all references and elements: `src/game/fund.ts` + `fund.test.ts`, the HUD/menus (`game.ts`, `index.html`, `style.css`), world props (`town.ts`, `hub.ts`), the `fund` net message (`protocol.ts`, `net.ts`, `server/room.ts`), `goal` fields in `maps.ts`, the save `fund` field (`state.ts`, `accounts.ts`, `server/index.ts`), tests, and `GAME_DESIGN.md`
  - Watch out: the server gate (`server/room.ts:262`) currently checks only the fund, and the level check happens only on the client (`game.ts:887`). Swap in a server-side `CASINO_LEVEL` check, or the casino ends up open to everyone server-side

- [x] Tab player list should show each player's level instead of their money
- [x] Luck items in the casino shop should read as "caught fish are worth x more"
  - The in-game text currently says "Every fish sells for +X%"
  - Source already says "worth" (commit fa24e73, 2026-10-17), but the built `dist/` bundle still says "sells for", so the running game may be a stale build. Rebuild (and redeploy if the live server is behind) and check before changing any code
- [x] Make scroll-wheel slot switching smooth
  - Right now it doesn't respond evenly to scroll movement and feels sticky; each notch/gesture should move one slot consistently
  - Add a slight animation when the highlight moves to a new hotbar slot
- [x] Allow changing inventory slots while sprinting
- [x] Remove the duplicated set of benches in the town square
  - Bench code lives in several places: `Town.benches()` around the well (`src/game/town.ts`), `Town.buildCozy()` (also `town.ts`), and the fire-pit benches in `src/game/hub.ts`. Confirm in-game which set is the extra one before removing it
- [x] Stop the player getting stuck on dock objects
  - Keep the decorative items sitting on the dock (bucket etc.) for the look, but don't let them block movement
  - Shore objects are fine as they are

- [x] Global chat in the top right
  - Non-intrusive, with a clear hint on how to use it ("Press Enter to chat")
  - Everyone on the server; filterable by world (pond vs casino) with Tab while in chat mode, if needed
  - No history on join: players only see messages sent after they log in (like OSRS)
  - Messages disappear from the chat window after 5 minutes
  - Standard max message length, spam/rate limiting, basic profanity filter
  - Chat bubble above the speaker's head, as long as it isn't obnoxious
- [x] Improve performance
  - Merged to `main` (2026-10-19): 23-29% faster rendering in every benchmark scene, original lighting kept. Results in `bench-results/ab-2a42083-vs-13dbd1f.json`
